h
and

\displaystyle|c_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{t_{0}^{\nu-1}(\log t_{0})^{m}}\|h\|_{0,\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}.

Proposition 6.2
.

Assume (
4.1
).
Let
0<\sigma<1
,
\epsilon>0
with
\sigma+\epsilon<\frac{3}{2}

and
1<\nu<\min(1+\frac{\epsilon}{2},3-\frac{\sigma}{2},\frac{5}{4})
.
Let
0<q<1
.
Then there is
C
such that for
t_{0}
large the following holds.
Suppose that
h
satisfies
\|h\|_{0,\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}<\infty
and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h(y,t)dy

\displaystyle=0,\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h(y,t)|y|^{2}dy=0,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h(y,t)y_{j}dy

\displaystyle=0,\quad j=1,2,\quad\text{for all }t>t_{0}.

Then there exists a solution
\phi(y,t)=\mathcal{T}^{i,1}_{\textbf{p}}[h]
of problem
(
6.1
)
that defines a linear operator of
h
and
satisfies

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},m+\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\leq C\|h\|_{0,\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}.

The proof of the Propositions
6.1
and
6.2
is divided into different steps and presented in sections
8
–
12
.

Next we consider the linear outer problem:

(6.4)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\phi^{o}&=L^{o}[\phi^{o}]+g(x,t),\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(t_{0},\infty)\\
\phi^{o}(\cdot,t_{0})&=\phi^{o}_{0},\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle L^{o}[\varphi]:=\Delta_{x}\varphi-\nabla_{x}\Big[\Gamma_{0}\Big(\frac{x-\xi(t)}{\lambda(t)}\Big)\Big]\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi.

For a given function
g(x,t)
we consider the norm
\|g\|_{**,o}
defined as the least
K\geq 0
such that for all

(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(t_{0},\infty)

(6.5)

\displaystyle|g(x,t)|\leq K\frac{1}{t^{a}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{1+|\zeta|^{b}},\quad\zeta=\frac{x-\xi(t)}{\sqrt{t}}.

Accordingly, we consider for a function
\phi^{o}(x,t)
the norm
\|\phi\|_{*,o}
defined as the least
K\geq 0
such that

(6.6)

\displaystyle|\phi^{o}(x,t)|+(\lambda+|x-\xi|)|{\nabla}_{x}\phi^{o}(x,t)|\leq K\frac{1}{t^{a-1}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{1+|\zeta|^{b}},\quad\zeta=\frac{x-\xi}{\sqrt{t}}

for all
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(t_{0},\infty)
.

We assume that the parameters
a,b,\beta
satisfy the constraints

(6.7)

\displaystyle 1<a<4,\quad 2<b<6,\quad a<1+\frac{b}{2},\quad\quad\beta\in\mathbb{R}.

Proposition 6.3
.

Assume that the parameter functions
\textbf{p}=(\lambda,\alpha,\xi)
satisfy conditions (
4.1
) and the numbers
a,b,\beta
satisfy (
6.7
).
Then there is a constant
C
so that for
t_{0}
sufficiently large and
for
\|g\|_{**,o}<\infty
,
there exists a solution
\phi^{o}=\mathcal{T}^{o}_{\textbf{p}}[g]
of (
6.4
) with
\phi^{o}_{0}=0
,
which defines a linear operator of
g
and satisfies

\displaystyle\|\phi^{o}\|_{*,o}\leq C\|g\|_{**,o}.

For the initial condition
\phi_{0}^{o}
in (
6.4
) we consider the norm
\|\varphi_{0}^{o}\|_{*,b}
defined as

\displaystyle\|\phi^{o}_{0}\|_{*,b}=

\displaystyle\inf K\quad\text{such that }

\displaystyle|\phi^{o}_{0}(x)|+(\lambda(t_{0})+|x|)|\nabla_{x}\phi^{o}(x)|\leq\frac{K}{(1+\frac{|x|}{\sqrt{t}})^{b}}.

We have an estimate for the solution of (
6.4
) with
g=0
and
\|\phi^{o}_{0}\|_{*,b}<\infty
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Assume that the parameter functions
\textbf{p}=(\lambda,\alpha,\xi)
satisfy conditions (
4.1
) and the numbers
a,b,\beta
satisfy (
6.7
).
Then there is a constant
C
so that for
t_{0}
sufficiently large and for
\|\phi^{o}_{0}\|_{*,b}<\infty
there exists a solution
\phi^{o}
of (
6.4
), which defines a linear operator of
\phi^{o}_{0}
and satisfies

\displaystyle\|\phi^{o}\|_{*,o}\leq Ct_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\beta}\|\phi^{o}_{0}\|_{*,b}.

The proofs of Propositions
6.3
and
6.4
are contained in Section
13
.

In what follows we work with
\textbf{p}_{1}
of the form

\textbf{p}_{1}=(0,\alpha_{1},\xi_{1}),

that is, we take
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
,
\alpha=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{1}
,
\xi=\xi_{1}
, where
\lambda_{0}
and
\alpha_{0}
have been fixed in Section
5.1
,
and we write

\textbf{p}=\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1}.

Next we define suitable operators that allow us to formulate
the system of equations (
5.47
), (
5.48
), (
5.49
), and (
5.51
) as a fixed point problem.
We let

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{i1}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1}]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}^{i,1}_{\textbf{p}}\Bigl[F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})

\displaystyle\quad-m_{0}[F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})]W_{0}-m_{2}[F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})]W_{2}

\displaystyle\quad-m_{1,1}[F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})]W_{1,1}-m_{1,2}[F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})]W_{1,2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{i2}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}^{i,2}_{\textbf{p}}\left[m_{2}[F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1}),\varphi_{0}^{*}]W_{2}\right]

\displaystyle A_{o}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}^{o}_{\textbf{p}}[G_{2}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})].

Then the equations (
5.47
), (
5.48
),(
5.49
) can be written as

\displaystyle\phi_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{i1}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]

\displaystyle\phi_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{i2}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]

\displaystyle\varphi

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{o}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}.]

Next we consider the equations (
5.51
), that is,
m_{0}[F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})](t)\equiv 0
and
m_{1,j}[F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})](t)\equiv 0
.
By (
5.50
) and (
5.41
)

\displaystyle m_{0}[F_{2}(\phi,\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi^{*}_{0})\tilde{\chi}]

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0}^{4}m_{0}[S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1}))-S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}))]+m_{0}[E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}]

\displaystyle\quad+m_{0}[F(\phi^{i}_{0}+\phi,\varphi^{*}+\varphi,\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})\tilde{\chi}]

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{0}^{4}m_{0}[(S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1}))-S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0})))(\tilde{\chi}-1)],

and using Lemma
5.3
,

\displaystyle m_{0}[F_{2}(\phi,\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi^{*}_{0})\tilde{\chi}]

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{0}^{2}\partial_{t}\Bigl\{\alpha_{1}\Bigl[8\pi\Bigl(1+2\Upsilon\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{t}\Bigr)+e_{1}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{t}\Bigr)\Bigr]+m_{0}[E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}]

\displaystyle\quad+m_{0}[F(\phi^{i}_{0}+\phi,\varphi^{*}+\varphi,\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})\tilde{\chi}]

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{0}^{4}m_{0}[(S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1}))-S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0})))(\tilde{\chi}-1)].

This motivates the definition

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{p,\alpha_{1}}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{1}{8\pi(1+2\Upsilon\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{t})+e_{1}(\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{t})}\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}^{2}}\Bigl\{m_{0}[E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}](s)+m_{0}[F(\phi^{i}_{0}+\phi,\varphi^{*}+\varphi,\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})\tilde{\chi}](s)

(6.8)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\lambda_{0}^{4}m_{0}[(S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1}))-S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0})))(\tilde{\chi}-1)](s)\Bigr\}ds

Similarly, by (
5.50
) and (
5.41
)

\displaystyle m_{1,j}[F_{2}(\phi,\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi^{*}_{0})\tilde{\chi}]

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0}\alpha\dot{\xi}_{1,j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{y_{j}}U(y)y_{j}\tilde{\chi}dy+\frac{\alpha\lambda_{0}^{2}}{\sqrt{t}}\dot{\xi}_{1,j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(y)\partial_{z_{j}}\chi_{0}(\frac{\lambda y}{\sqrt{t}})y_{j}dy

\displaystyle\quad+m_{1,j}[E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}]+m_{1,j}[F(\phi^{i}_{0}+\phi,\varphi^{*}+\varphi,\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})\tilde{\chi}].

This motivates the definition

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{p,\xi_{1}}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}\alpha\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{y_{j}}U(y)y_{j}\tilde{\chi}dy}\Bigl\{\frac{\alpha\lambda_{0}^{2}}{\sqrt{t}}\dot{\xi}_{1,j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(y)\partial_{z_{j}}\chi_{0}(\frac{\lambda y}{\sqrt{t}})y_{j}dy

(6.9)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+m_{1,j}[E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}](s)+m_{1,j}[F(\phi^{i}_{0}+\phi,\varphi^{*}+\varphi,\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})\tilde{\chi}](s)\Big\}ds

Then we define
\mathcal{A}_{p}
by

(6.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{p}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]=(0,\mathcal{A}_{p,\alpha_{1}}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}],\mathcal{A}_{p,\xi_{1}}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]).

Then

\mathbf{p}_{1}=\mathcal{A}_{p}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]

is equivalent to the equations (
5.51
).

We write

\vec{\phi}=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1}),

and

\mathcal{A}[\vec{\phi}]=(\mathcal{A}_{i1}[\vec{\phi},\varphi_{0}^{*}],\mathcal{A}_{i2}[\vec{\phi},\varphi_{0}^{*}],\mathcal{A}_{o}[\vec{\phi},\varphi_{0}^{*}],\mathcal{A}_{p}[\vec{\phi},\varphi_{0}^{*}]),

and the objective is to find
\vec{\phi}
such that

\vec{\phi}=\mathcal{A}[\vec{\phi}].

The operator
\mathcal{A}
depends on the initial condition
\varphi_{0}^{*}
appearing in the parabolic problem (
5.37
), and we will stress its dependence later on when proving the stability assertion in Theorem
1.1
.

We define the spaces on which we will consider the operator
\mathcal{A}
to set up the fixed point problem.
For certain choices of constants
\nu
,
q
,
\sigma
,
\epsilon
,
a
,
b
,
\beta
,
\gamma
,
\Upsilon

that we will make precise later,
we let

\displaystyle X_{i}=\Bigl\{\,\phi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(t_{0},\infty))\

\displaystyle|\ \nabla_{y}\phi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(t_{0},\infty)),\,\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}<\infty,

\displaystyle\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi(y,t)dy=0,\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi(y,t)ydy=0,\ \ t>t_{0}\,\Bigr\},

\displaystyle X_{o}=\{\varphi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(t_{0},\infty))\ |\ \nabla_{y}\phi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(t_{0},\infty)),\,\|\varphi\|_{*,o}<\infty\,\},

\displaystyle X_{p}=\{\,(0,\alpha_{1},\xi_{1})\in C^{1}([t_{0},\infty))\ |\ \|\alpha_{1}\|_{C^{1},\nu+\frac{1}{2},\Upsilon}<\infty,\ \|\xi_{1}\|_{C^{1},\gamma,0}<\infty\,\}

where the norms
\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}
and
\|\varphi\|_{*,o}
are defined in (
6.2
), (
6.5
) and
\|\xi_{1}\|_{C^{1},\mu,m}
is defined by

\displaystyle\|g\|_{C^{0},\mu,m}=\sup_{t\geq t_{0}}\,t^{\mu}(\log t)^{m}|g(t)|.

\displaystyle\|g\|_{C^{1},\mu,m}=\|g\|_{C^{0},\mu,m}+\|\dot{g}\|_{C^{0},\mu+1,m}.

for a function
g\in C^{1}([t_{0},\infty))
.

We choose in the definition of the outer norm (
6.6
)

(6.11)

\displaystyle a=\nu+\frac{5}{2},\quad 2\nu+3<b<6,\quad\beta<\frac{1+q}{2}.

With these choices we see that (
6.7
) are satisfied.
Also
\nu
will be in the range
1<\nu<\frac{3}{2}
so the interval for
b
is not empty in (
6.11
).

We use the following notation: for

\textbf{p}_{1}=(0,\alpha_{1},\xi_{1})

\displaystyle\|\textbf{p}_{1}\|_{X_{p}}=\|\alpha_{1}\|_{C^{1},\nu+\frac{1}{2},\Upsilon}+\|\xi_{1}\|_{C^{1},1+\gamma,0},

and for

\vec{\phi}=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1})

(6.12)

\displaystyle\|\vec{\phi}\|_{X}=\|\phi_{1}\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}+\|\phi_{2}\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}+\|\varphi\|_{*,o}+\|\textbf{p}_{1}\|_{X_{p}}.

With the above notation,
given
\varphi_{0}^{*}
with
\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}
sufficiently small,
we consider the fixed point problem

(6.13)

\displaystyle\vec{\phi}=\mathcal{A}[\vec{\phi}],

with
\vec{\phi}
in a suitable close ball of
X
.
A solution of this fixed point problem yields a solution of the system of equations (
5.47
), (
5.48
), (
5.49
), (
5.51
), which in turn gives a solution to (
3.1
).

We claim that for some constant
C
independent of
t_{0}\gg 1
, if

t_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\beta}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}\leq 1
,
and
\|\vec{\phi}\|_{X}\leq 1
, then

(6.14)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{i1}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}+C(\log t_{0})^{\frac{\sigma}{2}}t_{0}^{\nu+1+\frac{\sigma}{2}}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b},

for some
\vartheta>0
small, a constant
C
independent of
t_{0}
, and
t_{0}
sufficiently large.

Indeed, by Proposition
6.2
we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{i1}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\leq C\|F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})\|_{0,\nu,6+\sigma,\epsilon}.

We recall the expansion of
F_{3}
in (
5.50
). To estimate
E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}
we use (
5.43
) to get

(6.15)

\displaystyle\|E_{2}\tilde{\chi}\|_{0,\nu,0,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{1+2\delta-\frac{\sigma}{2}-\nu}(\log t_{0})^{2}}

where
\delta
,
\sigma
are positive small constants and are assumed to satisfy
2\delta-\frac{\sigma}{2}>0
. Then we take
\nu
in the range

(6.16)

\displaystyle 1<\nu<1+2\delta-\frac{\sigma}{2},

with
\nu
close to 1.

Let us consider the term
\lambda^{4}[S_{0}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})-S_{0}(\textbf{p}_{0})]
in
F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})
(c.f. (
5.50
)).
The formula
\lambda^{4}[S_{0}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})-S_{0}(\textbf{p}_{0})]
(c.f. (
5.31
)) contains for example the term, evaluated at
y=\frac{x-\xi_{1}}{\lambda_{0}}
,

\displaystyle-\lambda_{0}^{2}\dot{\alpha}U(y)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)+\lambda_{0}^{2}\dot{\alpha}_{0}U\Bigl(\frac{\xi_{1}+\lambda_{0}y}{\lambda_{0}}\Bigr)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\xi_{1}+\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad=-\lambda_{0}^{2}\dot{\alpha}_{1}U(y)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)-\lambda_{0}^{2}\dot{\alpha}_{0}\Big[U(y)-U\Bigl(\frac{\xi_{1}+\lambda_{0}y}{\lambda_{0}}\Bigr)\Bigr]\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)

(6.17)

\displaystyle\qquad-\lambda_{0}^{2}\dot{\alpha}_{0}U\Bigl(\frac{\xi_{1}+\lambda_{0}y}{\lambda_{0}}\Bigr)\Bigl[\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\xi_{1}+\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)-\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\Bigr]

But

\displaystyle\left|-\lambda_{0}^{2}\dot{\alpha}_{1}U(y)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{t^{\nu+\frac{1-\sigma}{2}}(\log t)^{\Upsilon-\frac{\sigma}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{6+\sigma}}\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\|\alpha_{1}\|_{C^{1},\nu+\frac{1}{2},\Upsilon},

so

\displaystyle\Bigl\|-\lambda_{0}^{2}\dot{\alpha}_{1}U(y)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\Bigr\|_{0,\nu,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}\|\alpha_{1}\|_{C^{1},\nu+\frac{1}{2},\Upsilon},

for some
\vartheta>0
.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\left|-\lambda_{0}^{2}\dot{\alpha}_{0}\Big[U(y)-U\Bigl(\frac{\xi_{1}+\lambda_{0}y}{\lambda_{0}}\Bigr)\Bigr]\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\log t}\frac{1}{t^{2}\log t}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{5}}\frac{|\xi_{1}|}{\lambda_{0}}\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{t^{2+\gamma}(\log t)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\frac{(t\log t)^{\frac{1+\sigma}{2}}}{(1+|y|)^{6+\sigma}}\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\|\xi_{1}\|_{C^{1},\gamma,0}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{t^{\frac{3-\sigma}{2}+\gamma}(\log t)^{1-\frac{\sigma}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{6+\sigma}}\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\|\xi_{1}\|_{C^{1},\gamma,0}

so

\displaystyle\Bigl\|-\lambda_{0}^{2}\dot{\alpha}_{0}\Big[U(y)-U\Bigl(\frac{\xi_{1}+\lambda_{0}y}{\lambda_{0}}\Bigr)\Bigr]\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{0}y}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\Bigr\|_{0,\nu,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}\|\alpha_{1}\|_{C^{1},\nu+\frac{1}{2},\Upsilon},

for some
\vartheta>0
.
The last term in the expression (
6.17
) is similar.

The terms in
\lambda^{4}[S_{0}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})-S_{0}(\textbf{p}_{0})]
that contain the function
\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}
are

\displaystyle{\lambda_{0}}^{4}\Bigl[-\frac{4}{r}\partial_{r}\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}-\nabla\cdot(\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}\nabla v_{0})-\nabla\cdot(u_{0}\nabla\psi_{\lambda_{0}})-\nabla\cdot(\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}\nabla\psi_{\lambda_{0}})\Bigr]

\displaystyle=4\frac{{\lambda_{0}}^{2}}{\rho(\rho^{2}+1)}\partial_{\rho}\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}-(\alpha-1){\lambda_{0}}^{2}\nabla_{y}\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}\cdot\nabla_{y}\Gamma_{0}+{\lambda_{0}}^{2}\nabla_{y}\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}\cdot\nabla_{y}\mathcal{R}+2{\lambda_{0}}^{2}U\chi\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad-\alpha\nabla_{y}(U\chi)\cdot\nabla_{y}\psi_{\lambda_{0}}-{\lambda_{0}}^{2}\nabla_{y}(\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}\nabla_{y}\psi_{\lambda_{0}}).

In
{\lambda_{0}}^{4}[S_{0}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})-S_{0}(\textbf{p}_{0})]

these terms appear evaluated at
y
and then at
\frac{\xi_{1}}{\lambda_{0}}+y
.
Using estimates for the the second derivative of
\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}
similar to Lemma
4.1
and assuming

(6.18)

\displaystyle\sigma<1,\quad\nu<1+\gamma,

we get

\displaystyle\|\lambda^{4}[S_{0}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})-S_{0}(\textbf{p}_{0})]\|_{0,\nu,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq C\frac{1}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}\|\vec{\phi}\|_{X}.

The main term in
F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})
that depends on the outer solution is
\lambda^{2}U\varphi^{o}

with
\varphi^{o}=\varphi^{*}+\varphi
defined in (
5.38
).
Then we have

\displaystyle|\lambda^{2}U\varphi(y,t)\tilde{\chi}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t^{a-1}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{4}}\tilde{\chi}\|\varphi\|_{*,o}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{t^{\nu+\frac{3}{2}}(\log t)^{\beta+1}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{4}}\tilde{\chi}\|\varphi\|_{*,o}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{(t\log t)^{1+\frac{\sigma}{2}}}{t^{\nu+\frac{3}{2}}(\log t)^{\beta+1}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{6+\sigma}}\tilde{\chi}\|\varphi\|_{*,o}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{t^{\nu+\frac{1-\sigma}{2}}(\log t)^{\beta-\frac{\sigma}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{6+\sigma}}\tilde{\chi}\|\varphi\|_{*,o}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\|\lambda^{2}U\varphi\tilde{\chi}\|_{0,\nu,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq C\frac{1}{t_{0}^{\frac{1-\sigma}{2}}(\log t_{0})^{\beta-\frac{\sigma}{2}}}\|\varphi\|_{*,o}.

Regarding the function
\varphi^{*}
(c.f. (
5.37
)) we note that it has the estimate

(6.19)

\displaystyle|\varphi^{*}(x,t)|\leq t_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\beta}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}\frac{1}{t^{a-1}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{1+|\zeta|^{b}},\quad\zeta=\frac{x-\xi}{\sqrt{t}}

by Proposition
6.4
, provided (
6.7
) holds, and therefore

\displaystyle\|\lambda^{2}U\varphi^{*}\tilde{\chi}\|_{0,\nu,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq Ct_{0}^{\nu+1+\frac{\sigma}{2}}(\log t_{0})^{\frac{\sigma}{2}}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}.

Let us analyze some of the terms in
F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})
that depend on the inner solutions
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
.
For instance

\displaystyle(\alpha-1)\nabla_{y}\cdot(\phi_{j}\nabla_{y}\Gamma_{0})=(\alpha-1)\nabla_{y}\phi_{j}\cdot\nabla_{y}\Gamma_{0}-(\alpha-1)\phi_{j}U.

We have the estimate

\displaystyle|(\alpha-1)\nabla_{y}\phi_{j}\cdot\nabla_{y}\Gamma_{0}\tilde{\chi}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t\log t}\frac{1}{t^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q-1}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{6}}\|\phi_{j}\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{t^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\sigma}{2}}(\log t)^{1+\frac{q-1}{2}-\frac{\sigma}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{6+\sigma}}\|\phi_{j}\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon},

and we get

\displaystyle\|(\alpha-1)\nabla_{y}\phi_{j}\cdot\nabla_{y}\Gamma_{0}\tilde{\chi}\|_{0,\nu,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}\|\phi_{j}\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon},

for some
\vartheta>0
.

We also have, writing
\phi=\phi_{1}+\phi_{2}
,

\|\lambda\dot{\xi}_{1}\nabla\phi\tilde{\chi}\|_{0,\nu,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}

for some
\vartheta>0
, if

(6.20)

\displaystyle\gamma>\frac{\sigma}{2}.

Let us estimate the term

\nabla_{y}\cdot(U\nabla_{y}(\hat{\psi}-\psi))\tilde{\chi}
appearing in (
5.6
), where
\hat{\psi}=(-\Delta)^{-1}(\lambda^{-2}\phi^{i}\chi)
,
\psi=(-\Delta)^{-1}(\lambda^{-2}\phi^{i})
. We recall that
\phi^{i}=\phi^{i}_{0}+\phi
, c.f. (
5.38
), and therefore we can decompose
\hat{\psi}=\hat{\psi}^{i}_{0}+\hat{\psi}_{1}
where
\hat{\psi}^{i}_{0}=(-\Delta)^{-1}(\lambda^{-2}\phi^{i}_{0}\chi)
and
\hat{\psi}_{1}=(-\Delta)^{-1}(\lambda^{-2}\phi\chi)
. Similarly, we can decompose
\psi=\psi^{i}_{0}+\psi_{1}
where
\psi^{i}_{0}=(-\Delta)^{-1}(\lambda^{-2}\phi^{i}_{0})
and
\psi_{1}=(-\Delta)^{-2}(\lambda^{-1}\phi)
.
By linearity we need to estimate separately

\nabla_{y}\cdot(U\nabla_{y}(\hat{\psi}^{i}_{0}-\psi^{i}_{0}))
and

\nabla_{y}\cdot(U\nabla_{y}(\hat{\psi}_{1}-\psi_{1}))
.
Let us consider the latter one.
Note that

\hat{\psi}_{1}-\psi_{1}=(-\Delta)^{-1}[\lambda^{-2}\phi(1-\chi)].

From the definition of the norm
\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}

(6.21)

\displaystyle|\phi(y,t)|\leq\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\frac{1}{t^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q-1}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{4}}

and so

|\nabla_{y}(\hat{\psi}_{1}-\psi_{1})(y,t)|\leq C\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\frac{1}{t^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q-1}{2}}}\frac{1}{(t\log t)^{\frac{3}{2}}},\quad\text{for }|y|\leq 2\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\lambda}.

Then

\displaystyle|\nabla_{y}U\cdot\nabla_{y}(\hat{\psi}_{1}-\psi_{1}))(y,t)|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\frac{1}{t^{\nu+\frac{1-\sigma}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q+1-\sigma}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{6+\sigma}},\quad\text{for }|y|\leq 2\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\lambda}.

This and a similar estimate for
U\phi(1-\chi)
give

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{y}\cdot(U\nabla_{y}(\hat{\psi}_{1}-\psi_{1}))\tilde{\chi}\|_{0,\nu,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}

for some
\vartheta>0
.
A similar estimate is obtained for
\|\nabla_{y}\cdot(U\nabla_{y}(\hat{\psi}^{i}_{0}-\psi^{i}_{0}))\tilde{\chi}\|_{0,\nu,6+\sigma,\epsilon}
using (
5.36
).

Let us estimate next the term
\lambda^{2}\nabla_{y}\cdot(\varphi_{\lambda}\nabla_{y}\psi)\tilde{\chi}
, where we recall,
\psi=(-\Delta)^{-1}(\lambda^{-2}\phi)
. To do this we use that
\phi=\phi_{1}+\phi_{2}
has zero mass and center of mass, that is,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi(y,t)\,dy=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi(y,t)y_{j}\,dy=0,\quad t>t_{0}.

This and the estimate (
6.21
) imply

\displaystyle|\psi(y,t)|+(1+|y|)|\nabla_{y}\psi(y,t)|\leq C\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\frac{1}{t^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q-1}{2}}}\frac{\log(2+|y|)}{(1+|y|)^{2}},

by an argument similar to Remark
9.1
.
On the other hand, from (
4.3
)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{y}\varphi_{\lambda}(y,t)|\leq\frac{C}{t\log t}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{3}},\quad|y|\leq 2\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\lambda}.

Therefore

\displaystyle|\lambda^{2}(\nabla_{y}\varphi_{\lambda}\cdot\nabla_{y}\psi)(y,t)|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q+1}{2}}}\frac{\log(2+|y|)}{(1+|y|)^{6}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\frac{1}{t^{\nu+\frac{1-\sigma}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q+1-\sigma}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{6+\sigma}},\quad|y|\leq 2\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\lambda}.

From this coupled with a similar estimate for
\lambda^{2}\varphi_{\lambda}\phi
we get

\displaystyle\|\lambda^{2}\nabla_{y}\cdot(\varphi_{\lambda}\nabla_{y}\psi)\tilde{\chi}\|_{0,\nu,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}

for some
\vartheta>0
.

The remaining terms in
F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})
are estimated in a similar way and we get the validity (
6.14
).

Proceeding in the same way we get a Lipschitz bound.

Assuming

t_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\beta}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}\leq 1
,
for
\|\vec{\phi}_{1}\|_{X}\leq 1
and
\|\vec{\phi}_{2}\|_{X}\leq 1
we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{i1}[\vec{\phi}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]-\mathcal{A}_{i1}[\vec{\phi}_{2},\varphi_{0}^{*}]\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}\|\vec{\phi}_{1}-\vec{\phi}_{2}\|_{X},

for some
\vartheta>0
small, a constant
C
independent of
t_{0}
, and
t_{0}
sufficiently large.
Indeed, the Lipschitz estimate with respect to
\phi_{1}
,
\phi_{2}
, and
\varphi
is direct from the explicit dependence of
F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})
on these variables, which is either linear or quadratic. The Lipschitz dependence on
\xi_{1}
(where
\textbf{p}_{1}=(\alpha_{1},\xi_{1}))
is also direct from the explicit form of
F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})
. The Lipschitz condition with respect to
\alpha_{1}
appears as an explicit dependence on this variable in
F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})
.

Let us estimate the operator
\mathcal{A}_{i2}
.
We claim that if
t_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\beta}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}\leq 1
and
\|\vec{\phi}\|_{X}\leq 1
, then

(6.22)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{i2}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\leq C(\log t_{0})^{-\frac{1-q}{2}-\Upsilon}+Ct_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\frac{1-q}{2}}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}.

Indeed, we apply Proposition
6.1
to get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{i2}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{(\log t_{0})^{1-q}}\left\|m_{2}[F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})]W_{2}\right\|_{0,\nu+\frac{1}{2},\frac{1-q}{2},6+\sigma,\epsilon}

and since
W_{2}
has compact support,

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{i2}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(\log t_{0})^{1-q}}\sup_{t>t_{0}}t^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{1-q}{2}}|m_{2}[F_{3}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})](t)|.

Using the definition of
F_{3}
(
5.50
)

\displaystyle m_{2}[F_{3}(\phi,\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})]=m_{2}[E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}]+m_{2}[F_{2}(\phi,\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})\tilde{\chi}]

We have by (
5.43
) (assuming
\sigma<\frac{1}{2}
),

\displaystyle|m_{2}[E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}](t)|\leq\frac{C}{t^{\frac{3+\sigma}{2}}}.

Therefore, asking that

(6.23)

\displaystyle\nu+\frac{1}{2}<\frac{3+\sigma}{2}\Leftrightarrow\nu<1+\frac{\sigma}{2}

we get

\displaystyle\sup_{t>t_{0}}t^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{1-q}{2}}|m_{2}[E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}](t)|\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}},

for some
\vartheta>0
.

By (
5.41
)

\displaystyle m_{2}[F_{2}(\phi,\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi^{*}_{0})\tilde{\chi}]

\displaystyle=\lambda^{4}m_{2}[S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1}))-S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}))]+m_{2}[F(\phi^{i}_{0}+\phi,\varphi^{*}+\varphi,\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})\tilde{\chi}]

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{4}m_{2}[(S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1}))-S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0})))(\tilde{\chi}-1)].

Of these terms, the largest is the first one.
By Lemma
5.4
, and since
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
, we get

\displaystyle\lambda^{4}m_{2}

\displaystyle[S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1}))-S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}))]

\displaystyle=-32\pi\alpha_{1}-\frac{\dot{\alpha}_{1}}{\lambda_{0}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(\frac{x-\xi}{\lambda_{0}})\chi_{0}(\frac{x-\xi}{\lambda_{0}})|x-\xi|^{2}\,dx

(6.24)

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}E(x-\xi,t,\lambda_{0})|x-\xi|^{2}\,dx-|\xi|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}S(u_{1}(\mathbf{p}_{0}))dx.

But

(6.25)

\displaystyle\sup_{t>t_{0}}t^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{1-q}{2}}|\alpha_{1}(t)|\leq C(\log t_{0})^{\frac{1-q}{2}-\Upsilon}\|\alpha_{1}\|_{C^{1},\nu+\frac{1}{2},\Upsilon},

under the assumption

(6.26)

\displaystyle\Upsilon>\frac{1-q}{2}.

The second term in (
6.24
) ia much smaller.
For the last term in (
6.24
) we use Lemma
5.3

and (
5.29
), (
5.30
) to get

(6.27)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}S(u_{1}(\mathbf{p}_{0}))dx\right|\leq\frac{C}{t^{2}}

and therefore

\displaystyle|\xi(t)|^{2}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}S(u_{1}(\mathbf{p}_{0}))dx\right|\leq\frac{C}{t^{2+2\gamma}}\|\xi_{1}\|_{C^{1},\gamma,0}^{2}.

Combining (
6.24
), (
6.25
) and (
6.27
) we get

\displaystyle\frac{C}{(\log t_{0})^{1-q}}\sup_{t>t_{0}}t^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{1-q}{2}}\lambda^{4}|m_{2}[S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1}))-S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}))](t)|\leq C(\log t_{0})^{-\frac{1-q}{2}-\Upsilon}\|\mathbf{p}_{1}\|_{X_{p}}.

Let’s estimate the remaining terms in
m_{2}[F_{3}(\phi,\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})]
. Consider

\displaystyle A(t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla_{y}\cdot(\lambda^{2}\varphi_{\lambda}\nabla_{y}(-\Delta_{y})^{-1}\phi)\tilde{\chi}|y|^{2}dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla_{y}\cdot(\phi\nabla_{y}\psi_{\lambda})\tilde{\chi}|y|^{2}dy

which appears in the definition of
F
, where
\phi=\phi_{1}+\phi_{2}
.
It is convenient to write

\displaystyle A(t)=A_{1}(t)-A_{2}(t)-A_{3}(t)

where

\displaystyle A_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla_{y}\cdot[(\lambda^{2}\varphi_{\lambda}+\phi)\nabla_{y}(\psi_{\lambda}+(-\Delta_{y})^{-1}\phi)]\tilde{\chi}|y|^{2}dy

\displaystyle A_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla_{y}\cdot[\lambda^{2}\varphi_{\lambda}\nabla_{y}\psi_{\lambda}]\tilde{\chi}|y|^{2}dy

\displaystyle A_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla_{y}\cdot[\phi\nabla_{y}\left((-\Delta_{y})^{-1}\phi\right)]\tilde{\chi}|y|^{2}dy.

We have, writing
\psi=(-\Delta_{y})^{-1}\phi
,

\displaystyle A_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Delta_{y}\psi\nabla_{y}\psi\cdot(|y|^{2}\nabla\tilde{\chi}+2\tilde{\chi}y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Delta_{y}\psi\nabla_{y}\psi\cdot y\Bigl[\frac{\lambda|y|}{2\sqrt{t}}\chi_{0}^{\prime}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda y}{2\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)+2\tilde{\chi}_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda y}{2\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\Bigr]dy.

Using Pohozaev’s identity

\displaystyle|A_{2}(t)|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla_{y}\Bigl[\nabla_{y}\psi(y\cdot\nabla\psi)-y\frac{|\nabla_{y}\psi|^{2}}{2}\Bigr]\Bigl[\frac{\lambda|y|}{2\sqrt{t}}\chi_{0}^{\prime}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda y}{2\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)+2\tilde{\chi}_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda y}{2\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\Bigr]dy\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Bigl[\nabla_{y}\psi(y\cdot\nabla\psi)-y\frac{|\nabla_{y}\psi|^{2}}{2}\Bigr]\cdot\nabla_{y}\Bigl[\frac{\lambda|y|}{2\sqrt{t}}\chi_{0}^{\prime}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda y}{2\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)+2\tilde{\chi}_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda y}{2\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\Bigr]dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{2\sqrt{t}/\lambda\leq|y|\leq 4\sqrt{t}/\lambda}|\nabla_{y}\psi|^{2}dy.

Using that
\psi=(-\Delta)^{-1}\phi
, and

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi(y,t)dy=0,\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi(y,t)ydy=0,

we have (see Remark
9.1
) for any
\varrho>0
small,

\displaystyle|\nabla\psi(y,t)|\leq\frac{C}{1+|y|^{2-\varrho}}\frac{1}{t^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q-1}{2}}}\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{2\sqrt{t}/\lambda\leq|y|\leq 4\sqrt{t}/\lambda}|\nabla_{y}\psi|^{2}dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t^{2\nu-\varrho}(\log t)^{q-\varrho}}\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}^{2}

and so

\displaystyle|A_{2}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}+\vartheta}}.

The estimates of the other terms,
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
is similar, except that
\varphi_{\lambda}
doesn’t have mass equal to zero. Instead, we use that
\varphi_{\lambda}
, and
\psi_{\lambda}
are radial and

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda}dx\right|\leq\frac{C}{t\log t},

by Lemmas
7.1
and
7.4
, to get

|\psi_{\lambda}(y,t)|\,\leq\,\frac{C}{t\sqrt{\log t}}\frac{1}{1+|y|}

Let us consider the contribution of the term
\lambda^{2}U\varphi^{*}
. Thanks to (
6.19
)

\displaystyle\|m_{2}[\lambda^{2}U\varphi^{*}\tilde{\chi}]W_{2}\|_{0,\nu+\frac{1}{2},\frac{1-q}{2},6+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq Ct_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\frac{1-q}{2}}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b},

under the condition

(6.28)

\displaystyle\beta>\frac{1-q}{2}.

The other terms in
m_{2}
are estimated in a similar way and we get (
6.22
).

Similarly we get that
if
t_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\beta}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}\leq 1
, then
for
\|\vec{\phi}_{1}\|_{X}\leq 1
and
\|\vec{\phi}_{2}\|_{X}\leq 1
we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{i2}[\vec{\phi}_{1}]-\mathcal{A}_{i2}[\vec{\phi}_{2}]\|_{0,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\leq C(\log t_{0})^{-\frac{1-q}{2}-\Upsilon}\|\vec{\phi}_{1}-\vec{\phi}_{2}\|_{X},

for a constant
C
independent of
t_{0}
, where
t_{0}
sufficiently large.

Let us estimate the operator
\mathcal{A}_{o}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]
.
We claim that if
t_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\beta}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}\leq 1
, then for
\|\vec{\phi}\|_{X}\leq 1
,

(6.29)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{o}[\vec{\phi},\varphi_{0}^{*}]\|_{*,o}\leq\frac{C}{(\log t_{0})^{\frac{q+1}{2}-\beta}}+Ct_{0}^{a-2}(\log t_{0})^{\beta-1}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b},

and for
\|\vec{\phi}_{1}\|_{X}\leq 1
,
\|\vec{\phi}_{2}\|_{X}\leq 1

and
t_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\beta}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{o}[\vec{\phi}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]-\mathcal{A}_{o}[\vec{\phi}_{2},\varphi_{0}^{*}]\|_{*,o}\leq\frac{C}{(\log t_{0})^{\frac{q+1}{2}-\beta}}\|\vec{\phi}_{1}-\vec{\phi}_{2}\|_{X}.

Note that
\frac{q+1}{2}-\beta>0
by (
6.11
).

Indeed, by Proposition
6.3

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{o}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]\|_{*,o}\leq C\|G_{2}(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*})\|_{**,o},

where we recall
G_{2}
defined in (
5.42
).

We start with the term
\lambda^{-4}E_{2}(1-\tilde{\chi}_{2})\chi
. Using the estimate (
5.43
) we get

\displaystyle\|\lambda^{-4}E_{2}(1-\tilde{\chi}_{2})\chi\|_{**,o}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}

for some
\vartheta>0
provided

a<4(1-\delta).

We also directly get from (
4.6
)

\|S(u_{1})(1-\chi)\|_{**,o}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}

for some
\vartheta>0
if
a<4
.

Regarding the terms in
G
(c.f. (
5.10
)) that the depend linearly on
\phi^{i}=\phi^{i}_{0}+\phi
, we have for
\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}<\infty

\displaystyle\Bigl|\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\phi\Delta\chi\Bigr|(x,t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\lambda^{2}}\frac{1}{t^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q-1}{2}}}\frac{1}{(|x-\xi|/\lambda|)^{4}}\frac{1}{t}\Bigl|\Delta_{z}\chi_{0}(\frac{x-\xi}{\sqrt{t}})\Bigr|\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}

(6.30)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{t^{\nu+\frac{5}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q+1}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+|x-\xi|/\sqrt{t})^{b}}\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}

which implies

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\phi\Delta\chi\Bigr\|_{**,o}\leq\frac{C}{(\log t_{0})^{\frac{q+1}{2}-\beta}}\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon},

since
\beta<\frac{q+1}{2}
, which is one of the conditions in (
6.11
).

We also have, using (
5.36
),

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\phi_{0}^{i}\Delta\chi\Bigr\|_{**,o}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}

for some
\vartheta>0
if

\displaystyle a<4.

A similar estimate holds for the other terms depending on
\phi^{i}
.

Some of the terms in
G
that depend on
\varphi^{o}=\varphi^{*}+\varphi
are

\displaystyle\Bigl|\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}U\varphi^{o}(1-\chi)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\lambda^{2}}{|x-\xi|^{4}}\frac{1}{t^{a-1}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{(1+|x-\xi|/\sqrt{t})^{b}}(1-\chi)\|\varphi^{o}\|_{*,o}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}\log t_{0}}\frac{1}{t^{a}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{(1+|x-\xi|/\sqrt{t})^{b}}(\|\varphi^{*}\|_{*,o}+\|\varphi\|_{*,o})

which implies that

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}U\varphi^{o}(1-\chi)\Bigr\|_{**,o}\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}\log t_{0}}\|\varphi\|_{*,o}+Ct_{0}^{a-2}(\log t_{0})^{\beta-1}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b},

by Proposition
6.4
.
Other terms are estimated in a similar way.

Let us estimate the operator
\mathcal{A}_{p}
, which is defined by the equations (
6.10
).
We claim that if

(0,\tilde{\alpha}_{1},\tilde{\xi}_{1})=\mathcal{A}_{p}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1}]

and
t_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\beta}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}\leq 1
,
\|\vec{\phi}\|_{X}\leq 1
,
\vec{\phi}=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\mathbf{p}_{1})
, then

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\alpha}_{1}\|_{C^{1},\nu+\frac{1}{2},\Upsilon}

\displaystyle\leq C(\log t_{0})^{\Upsilon-\beta}+Ct_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\Upsilon}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}

(6.31)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\xi}_{1}\|_{*,\gamma,0}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t_{0}^{\vartheta}}+Ct_{0}^{1+\gamma}(\log t_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b},

for some
\vartheta>0
.
Similarly, we have the following Lipschitz estimate. If
t_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\beta}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}\leq 1
, then for some
\vartheta>0
, and for
\|\vec{\phi}_{1}\|_{X}\leq 1
,
\|\vec{\phi}_{2}\|_{X}\leq 1
,

(6.32)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{p}[\vec{\phi}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]-\mathcal{A}_{p}[\vec{\phi}_{2},\varphi_{0}^{*}]\|_{X_{p}}\leq C(\log t_{0})^{\Upsilon-\beta}\|\vec{\phi}_{1}-\vec{\phi}_{2}\|_{X},

for some
\vartheta>0
.

Indeed, by (
6.8
)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{A}_{p,\alpha_{1}}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}](t)|\leq|I_{1}(t)|+|I_{2}(t)|+|I_{3}(t)|

where

\displaystyle I_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}^{2}}m_{0}[E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}](s)ds

\displaystyle I_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}^{2}}m_{0}[F(\phi^{i}_{0}+\phi,\varphi^{*}+\varphi,\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})\tilde{\chi}](s)ds

\displaystyle I_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{\infty}\lambda_{0}^{2}m_{0}[(S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1}))-S_{0}(u_{1}(\textbf{p}_{0})))(\tilde{\chi}-1)](s)ds.

Using (
5.43
) and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}E_{2}dy=0
we get

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}^{2}}m_{0}[E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}](t)\right|\leq C\frac{1}{t^{3-2\delta}}.

This gives

(6.33)

\displaystyle\|I_{1}\|_{C^{1},\nu+\frac{1}{2},\Upsilon}\leq Ct_{0}^{\nu-\frac{3}{2}+2\delta},

under the assumption

\displaystyle\nu<\frac{3}{2}-2\delta.

The largest contribution in
I_{2}
comes from the term
\lambda^{2}U\varphi^{o}
in
F(\phi^{i}_{0}+\phi,\varphi^{*}+\varphi,\textbf{p}_{0}+\textbf{p}_{1})
(c.f. (
5.6
)).
The estimate of this term is

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}^{2}(t)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\lambda_{0}(t)^{2}U(y)\varphi^{o}(y,t)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{t^{\nu+\frac{3}{2}}(\log t)^{\beta}}\|\varphi^{o}\|_{*,o}

and so

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(y)\varphi^{o}(y,t)dy\Bigr\|_{C^{1},\nu+\frac{1}{2},\Upsilon}\leq C(\log t_{0})^{\Upsilon-\beta}\|\varphi^{o}\|_{*,o},

under the assumption

(6.34)

\displaystyle\Upsilon<\beta.

Similar estimates for the remaining terms give

(6.35)

\displaystyle\|I_{2}\|_{C^{1},\nu+\frac{1}{2},\Upsilon}\leq C(\log t_{0})^{\Upsilon-\beta}\|\vec{\phi}\|_{X}+Ct_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\Upsilon}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}.

Regarding
I_{3}
, using (
4.5
) we have

(6.36)

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}^{2}m_{0}[S_{0}(u_{1}(\mathbf{p}))(\tilde{\chi}-1)]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t^{3}\log t}.

Putting together (
6.33
), (
6.35
), and (
6.36
) we get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{p,\alpha_{1}}[\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\varphi,\textbf{p}_{1},\varphi_{0}^{*}]\|_{C^{1},\nu+\frac{1}{2},\Upsilon}\leq C(\log t_{0})^{\Upsilon-\beta}\|\vec{\phi}\|_{X}+Ct_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\Upsilon}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}

assuming also that

\displaystyle\nu<\frac{3}{2}.

The computations leading to (
6.31
) are very similar, under the assumption

(6.37)

\displaystyle\gamma<\nu-\frac{1}{2}.

This restriction arises when considering the largest term in the expression (
6.9
), namely comes from estimating the term
\lambda_{0}^{2}m_{1,j}[\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}\phi\tilde{\chi}]
(
\lambda_{0}^{2}\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}\phi
is one of the terms in (
5.6
))

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}}\lambda_{0}^{2}|m_{1,j}[\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}\phi\tilde{\chi}](t)|

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}\phi y_{j}|dy

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda_{0}\frac{1}{t(\log t)^{2}}\frac{1}{t^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q-1}{2}}}\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}

Let us summarize the restrictions on the parameters.
We let
0<q<1
be fixed. We take

\displaystyle 0<\delta<\sigma<\min(1,4\delta),

and

\displaystyle 1<\nu<\min\Bigl(1+2\delta-\frac{\sigma}{2},\frac{3}{2},1+\gamma,1+\frac{\sigma}{2}\Bigr).

because of (
6.16
), (
6.18
), (
6.23
).
We also need

\displaystyle\frac{1-q}{2}<\Upsilon<\beta<\frac{1+q}{2}

by (
6.26
), (
6.34
) and by (
6.28
) and (
6.11
).
We take

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma}{2}<\gamma<\nu-\frac{1}{2}

by (
6.20
) and (
6.37
).

Together with the above inequalities we want also the relations

\sigma+\epsilon<2
,
\nu+\frac{1}{2}<\frac{7}{4}
for Proposition
6.1
and
\sigma+\epsilon<\frac{3}{2}
,
\nu<\min(1+\frac{\epsilon}{2},3-\frac{\sigma}{2},\frac{5}{4})
for Proposition
6.2
. The condition (
6.7
) for Propositions
6.3
and
6.4
hold by (
6.11
). We see that all these restrictions are satisfied by choosing first
\delta
,
\sigma>0
small so that
2\delta-\frac{\sigma}{2}>0
. Then we take
\nu>1
close to 1, then let
a=\nu+\frac{5}{2}
and
b
satisfying (
6.11
).
Then
\Upsilon
,
\beta
and
\gamma
can be selected.
Note that with the above procedure we are getting the restriction
b>5
.

We already have all elements to solve the fixed point problem (
6.13
), which we recall

\displaystyle\vec{\phi}=\mathcal{A}[\vec{\phi}],\quad\vec{\phi}\in\mathcal{B},

where
\mathcal{B}
is the closed
unit ball in the Banach space of
functions
\vec{\phi}
with

\|\vec{\phi}\|_{X}<+\infty
and the norm defined in (
6.12
). Thus

\mathcal{B}=\{\,\vec{\phi}\in X\,|\,\|\vec{\phi}\|_{X}\leq 1\,\}.

Let
\varphi_{0}^{*}
be such that
t_{0}^{a-1}(\log t_{0})^{\beta}\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}\leq 1
.
Estimates (
6.14
), (
6.22
), (
6.29
) and (
6.32
), imply that, enlarging the parameter
t_{0}
if necessary,

\mathcal{A}
maps
\mathcal{B}
into itself.
We also get that
\mathcal{A}
is a contraction mapping on
\mathcal{B}
.
The contraction mapping principle yields
the existence of a unique fixed point in
\mathcal{B}
, which then yields the required existence result.

Remark 6.1
.

The computations above provide the estimates necessary to show that
\mathcal{A}
has a fixed point on
\mathcal{B}
. We show here why the scheme works restricting our attention to the most delicate terms only.
We adopt a strategy slightly different to that of finding a fixed point of
\mathcal{A}
:
we consider the inner equations (
5.47
) and (
5.48
) as a system where
\varphi
is an operator of
\phi=\phi_{1}+\phi_{2}
, which is found by solving the outer problem (
5.49
).
In the system (
5.47
) and (
5.48
) we regard
\alpha_{1}
and
\xi_{1}
as operators of
\phi
,
\varphi[\phi]
given by the formulas
\alpha_{1}=\mathcal{A}_{p,\alpha_{1}}
defined in (
6.8
) and
\xi_{1}=\mathcal{A}_{p,\xi_{1}}
defined in (
6.9
).

It is natural so use the same norm for
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
, since it is
\phi=\phi_{1}+\phi_{2}
that appears in the right hand side of the outer problem (
5.49
). Then estimate (
6.15
) and Proposition
6.2
suggest that

\displaystyle\|\phi_{j}\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\varepsilon}<\infty,

where we have ignored all other terms in (
5.47
).
The term
\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\phi\Delta\chi
in (
5.49
), the computation in (
6.30
) and Proposition
6.3
give that

(6.38)

\displaystyle|\varphi(x,t)|\leq C\frac{1}{t^{\nu+\frac{3}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q+1}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+|x-\xi|/\sqrt{t})^{b}}[\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\varepsilon}+C(\varphi_{0}^{*})],

where
C(\varphi_{0}^{*})
denotes a constant that depends on the initial condition
\varphi_{0}^{*}
, and whose exact form we don’t need now.

Considering
\varphi
as an operator of
\phi
we examine the effect of the therm
\lambda^{2}U\varphi
. This term appears in the right hand side of (
5.47
), where the effect is less important, and in the computation of
\alpha_{1}
. Ignoring all but this term, we find from (
6.38
) the estimate

\displaystyle|\alpha_{1}[\phi](t)|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(y)|\varphi(\xi+\lambda y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{t^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q+1}{2}}}[\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\varepsilon}+C(\varphi_{0}^{*})].

We consider now the effect of
|\alpha_{1}[\phi](t)|
in the right hand side of (
5.48
), where thanks to Lemma
5.4
appears mainly as
\alpha_{1}(t)W_{2}(y)
, where
W_{2}
is radial with compact support. Then Proposition
6.1
gives

\displaystyle|\phi_{2}(y,t)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{(\log t_{0})^{1-q}}\frac{1}{t^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log t)^{\frac{q+1}{2}+q-1}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{4}}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{(t\log t)^{1/2}}{|y|}\Bigr)^{2+\sigma+\epsilon}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot[\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\varepsilon}+C(\varphi_{0}^{*})].

Because we want to use the norm
\|\ \|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\varepsilon}
we get

\displaystyle\|\phi_{2}\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\varepsilon}\leq C\frac{1}{\log t_{0}}[\epsilon_{e}+\|\phi\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\varepsilon}+C(\varphi_{0}^{*})].

The
\epsilon_{e}>0
on the right hand is there because of the term
E_{2}\tilde{\chi}_{2}
in equation (
5.39
). It can be chosen to be a small constant taking
t_{0}
large.
The estimate for
\phi_{1}
is actually better, and therefore

\displaystyle\|\phi_{1}\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\varepsilon}+\|\phi_{2}\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C\frac{1}{\log t_{0}}[\|\phi_{1}\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\varepsilon}+\|\phi_{2}\|_{1,\nu-\frac{1}{2},\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\varepsilon}+C(\varphi_{0}^{*})].

This indicates that the problem for
\phi_{1}
,
\phi_{2}
may be solved by the contraction mapping principle.

Stability

If we solve system (
5.47
), (
5.48
), (
5.49
), (
5.51
) for a given
\varphi_{0}^{*}
and write
\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{p}(\varphi_{0}^{*})
, we have a solution of problem (
3.1
), which blows up in infinite time as described in Theorem
1.1
, with initial condition

\displaystyle u^{*}(x,;\varphi_{0}^{*})

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*})}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})^{2}}\Big[U\Bigl(\frac{x-\xi(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*})}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})}\Bigr)+\phi_{0}^{i}\Bigl(\frac{x-\xi(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*})}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})}\Bigr)+c_{1}(\varphi_{0}^{*})\tilde{Z}_{0}\Bigl(\frac{x-\xi(t_{0})}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})}\Bigr)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{x-\xi(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*})}{\sqrt{t_{0}}}\Bigr)+\tilde{\varphi}_{\lambda_{0}}(x-\xi(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*}),t_{0})+\varphi_{0}^{*}(x).

where we recall that
\tilde{\varphi}_{\lambda}
was defined in (
3.8
) and
\varphi_{\lambda}(x,t)=\tilde{\varphi}_{\lambda}(x-\xi(t),t)
.
The function
\tilde{\varphi}
doesn’t depend on
\xi
and is radial about the origin.

We let
u_{0}^{*}(x)=u^{*}(x;0)
and

\displaystyle I_{0}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u_{0}^{*}(x)|x|^{2}dx.

Note that
u_{0}^{*}
is radial and so it has center of mass at the origin.

To prove stability we first show the following claim:
if
v:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies
\|v\|_{*,b}<t_{0}^{1-a}(\log t_{0})^{-\beta}
, has mass zero, and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}v(x)x_{j}dx=0,\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}v(x)|x|^{2}dx=0,\quad

then
u_{0}^{*}+v=u^{*}(\varphi_{0}^{*})
for some
\varphi_{0}^{*}
with mass zero and
\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}<t_{0}^{1-a}(\log t_{0})^{-\beta}
. Indeed, the equation for
\varphi_{0}^{*}
has the form

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*})}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})^{2}}\Big[U\Bigl(\frac{x-\xi(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*})}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})}\Bigr)+\phi_{0}^{i}\Bigl(\frac{x-\xi(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*})}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})}\Bigr)+c_{1}(\varphi_{0}^{*})\tilde{Z}_{0}\Bigl(\frac{x-\xi(t_{0})}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})}\Bigr)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{x-\xi(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*})}{\sqrt{t_{0}}}\Bigr)+\tilde{\varphi}_{\lambda_{0}}(x-\xi(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*}),t_{0})+\varphi_{0}^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha(t_{0};0)}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})^{2}}\Big[U\Bigl(\frac{x}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})}\Bigr)+\phi_{0}^{i}\Bigl(\frac{x}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})}\Bigr)+c_{1}(0)\tilde{Z}_{0}\Bigl(\frac{x}{\lambda_{0}(t_{0})}\Bigr)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{x}{\sqrt{t_{0}}}\Bigr)+\varphi_{\lambda_{0}}(x,t_{0})+v.

A solution to this equation is
\varphi_{0}^{*}=v
. Indeed, with this choice, computing the center of mass we find that
\xi(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*})=0
, computing the mass we find that
\alpha(t_{0};\varphi_{0}^{*})=\alpha(t_{0};0)
and computing the second moment we obtain
c_{1}(\varphi_{0}^{*})=c_{1}(0)
.

Now consider a general
v
with
\|v\|_{*,b}<\frac{1}{M}t_{0}^{1-a}(\log t_{0})^{-\beta}
and mass zero (where
M>0
is a constant to be chosen).
We want to show that the initial condition
u_{0}^{*}+v
produces a solution to (
3.1
) with infinite time blow as described in Theorem
1.1
.
To prove this, consider

\displaystyle u_{\Lambda,p}(x)=\frac{1}{\Lambda^{2}}\Bigl[u_{0}^{*}\Bigl(\frac{x-p}{\Lambda}\Bigr)+v\Bigl(\frac{x-p}{\Lambda}\Bigr)\Bigr],

where
p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\Lambda>0
.
Note that
u_{\Lambda,p}
has mass
8\pi
.
Then we select
\Lambda
and
p
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u_{\Lambda,p}(x)x_{j}dx=0,\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u_{\Lambda,p}(x)|x|^{2}dx=I_{0}.

Note that
|\Lambda-1|\leq\frac{C}{M}t_{0}^{1-a}(\log t_{0})^{-\beta}
and
|p|\leq\frac{C}{M}t_{0}^{1-a}(\log t_{0})^{-\beta}
.
Then we expand

\displaystyle u_{\Lambda,p}(x)=u_{0}^{*}+w

and
w
satisfies
\|w\|_{*,b}\leq\frac{C}{M}t_{0}^{1-a}(\log t_{0})^{-\beta}
, has mass zero, center of mass zero and second moment equal to
I_{0}
.
Choosing
M=C
, by the previous claim, there is
\varphi_{0}^{*}
with
\|\varphi_{0}^{*}\|_{*,b}<t_{0}^{1-a}(\log t_{0})^{-\beta}
such that
u_{\Lambda_{p}}=u^{*}(\varphi_{0}^{*})
. The initial condition
u^{*}(\varphi_{0}^{*})
is such that the solution to (
3.1
) blows up as in Theorem
1.1
. Then the same is true for the initial condition
u_{0}^{*}+v
after a scaling and translation in space.

7.
The mass of
\varphi_{\lambda}

We devote this section to prove Proposition
5.1
. To that purpose,
a basic step is to derive a formula for the mass of
\varphi_{\lambda}
defined in (
3.11
).

Let us write

(7.1)

\displaystyle\varphi_{\lambda}=\varphi_{\lambda}^{(1)}+\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}

where
\varphi_{\lambda}^{(1)}
and
\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}
are the solutions, given by Duhamel’s formula, of the following problems

(7.2)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\varphi_{\lambda}^{(1)}&=\Delta_{6}\varphi_{\lambda}^{(1)}+\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda^{3}}Z_{0}(\frac{x}{\lambda})\chi_{0}(z)\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times({\textstyle\frac{t_{0}}{2}},\infty)\\
\varphi_{\lambda}^{(1)}(\cdot,{\textstyle\frac{t_{0}}{2}})&=0\end{aligned}\right.

(7.3)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}&=\Delta_{6}\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{2\lambda^{2}t}U\nabla_{z}\chi_{0}(z)\cdot z+\tilde{E},\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times({\textstyle\frac{t_{0}}{2}},\infty),\quad z=\frac{x}{\sqrt{t}},\\
\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}(\cdot,{\textstyle\frac{t_{0}}{2}})&=0\end{aligned}\right.

where the operator
\Delta_{6}
is defined in (
3.7
) and
\tilde{E}
in (
3.10
).
We let
\varphi[p,\lambda](r,t)
be the solution of the problem

(7.4)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\varphi[p,\lambda]&=\Delta_{6}\varphi[p,\lambda]+\frac{p}{\lambda^{4}}Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{r}{\lambda}\Bigr)\chi\Bigr(\frac{r}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigl)\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times({\textstyle\frac{t_{0}}{2}},\infty),\\
\varphi[p,\lambda](\cdot,{\textstyle\frac{t_{0}}{2}})&=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2},\end{aligned}\right.

given by Duhamel’s formula.
By definition, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{\lambda}^{(1)}=\varphi[\lambda\dot{\lambda},\lambda].

In definitions (
7.2
), (
7.3
), (
7.4
), the parameter function
\lambda(t)
is assumed to be defined for
t>\frac{t_{0}}{2}
.
In the rest of this section we also assume the validity of the condition stated for
\lambda
in (
4.1
), namely

(7.5)

\displaystyle|\lambda(t)|+t\log(t)|\dot{\lambda}(t)|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{\log(t)}},\quad t>\frac{t_{0}}{2},

for some fixed constant
C
.
Let us define

(7.6)

\displaystyle\|p\|_{\gamma,m}=\sup_{t\geq t_{0}/2}\,t^{\gamma}(\log t)^{m}|p(t)|.

In what follows we shall only deal with radial functions on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and sometimes we will consider them as radial functions on
\mathbb{R}^{6}
.
For a fixed constant
c_{0}>0

we let

(7.7)

\displaystyle\lambda^{*}(t)=\frac{c_{0}}{\sqrt{\log t}}.

The following expansion holds.

Lemma 7.1
.

Assume that
\lambda
satisfies (
7.5
).
Let
0<\gamma<2
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
and suppose that
\|p\|_{\gamma,m}<\infty
.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi[p,\lambda](x)dx=-4\pi\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)}{t-s}ds+R[p,\lambda]

where
R[p,\lambda]
satisfies

\|R[p,\lambda]\|_{\gamma,m}\leq C\|p\|_{\gamma,m}.

If
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
satisfy

\Bigl\|\frac{\lambda_{j}}{\lambda^{*}}\Bigl\|_{L^{\infty}(t_{0}/2,\infty)}<\frac{1}{2},\quad j=1,2,

then we also have

(7.8)

\displaystyle\|R[p,\lambda^{*}+\lambda_{1}]-R[p,\lambda^{*}+\lambda_{2}]\|_{\gamma,m}\leq C\|p\|_{\gamma,m}\Bigl\|\frac{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}{\lambda^{*}}\Bigl\|_{L^{\infty}(t_{0}/2,\infty)}.

For the proof of the above result we will need the following calculation.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let

\displaystyle f(w)=\frac{1}{(4\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}e^{-\frac{|z|^{2}}{4}}\frac{1}{|w-z|^{4}}dz,\quad w\in\mathbb{R}^{6}.

Then

(7.9)

\displaystyle f(w)=\frac{1}{|w|^{4}}\Bigl[1-e^{\frac{-|w|^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{|w|^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr].

Proof.

Let
\varphi_{0}
be given by

\varphi_{0}(x,t)=\frac{1}{(4\pi)^{3}}\frac{1}{t^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{4t}}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{4}}dy,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{6},\ t>0,

which solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\varphi_{0}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\varphi_{0}\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{6}\times(0,\infty)

\displaystyle\varphi_{0}(x,0)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|x|^{4}}.

Then

f(w)=\varphi_{0}(w,1).

Write

\displaystyle\varphi_{0}(x,t)=\frac{1}{t^{2}}q\Bigl(\frac{|x|}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr).

Then

q^{\prime\prime}(s)+\frac{5}{s}q^{\prime}(s)+\frac{s}{2}q^{\prime}(s)+2q(s)=0

and we want
q(s)
bounded for
s\to 0
,
q(s)=s^{-4}(1+o(1))
as
s\to\infty
.
A calculation using the explicit element in the kernel of the linear operator,
s^{-4}
, gives

q(s)=\frac{1}{s^{4}}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{s^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr],\quad s>0,

and then (
7.9
) follows.

∎

Proof of Lemma
7.1
.

The solution
\varphi[p,\lambda]
of (
7.4
) has the formula

\displaystyle\varphi[p,\lambda](x,t)=\frac{1}{(4\pi)^{3}}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t}\frac{p(s)}{\lambda^{4}(s)}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{y}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\chi\Bigr(\frac{y}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)dyds,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{6}.

Writing

\displaystyle\varphi=\varphi[p,\lambda]

we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi(x,t)\,dx

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\varphi(x,t)|x|^{-4}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi^{2}}\frac{1}{(4\pi)^{3}}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t}\frac{p(s)}{\lambda^{4}(s)}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}|x|^{-4}dxZ_{0}\Bigl(\frac{y}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\chi\Bigr(\frac{y}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)dyds

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi^{2}}\frac{1}{(4\pi)^{3}}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t}\frac{p(s)}{\lambda(s)^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}e^{-\frac{|z|^{2}}{4}}\frac{1}{|y-\sqrt{t-s}z|^{4}}dzZ_{0}\Bigl(\frac{y}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\chi\Bigr(\frac{y}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)dyds

Using (
7.9
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi(x,t)dx

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi^{2}}\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t}\frac{p(s)}{\lambda(s)^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\frac{1}{(t-s)^{2}}f((t-s)^{-1/2}|y|)Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{y}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\chi\Bigr(\frac{y}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)dyds

\displaystyle=2\pi\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t}\frac{p(s)}{\lambda(s)^{4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{4(t-s)}}\Bigl(1+\frac{r^{2}}{4(t-s)}\Bigr)\Bigr]Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{r}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\chi\Bigr(\frac{r}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)rdrds.

Let us notice that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi(x,t)dx

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t}\frac{p(s)(t-s)}{\lambda(s)^{4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\chi\Bigr(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)zdzds.

We decompose

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi(x,t)dx

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}

where

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}...

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}...

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}^{t}...

and separately estimate each term.
To estimate
I_{1}
we note that for
s\leq t/2
we have
\frac{s}{t-s}\leq 1
.
Assuming that
\chi(x)=0
for
x\geq 2
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\chi\Bigr(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)zdz

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2\frac{\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{t-s}}}...

We estimate for
s\leq t/2
,

\displaystyle\Bigl|\int_{0}^{2\frac{\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{t-s}}}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\chi\Bigr(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)zdz\Bigr|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\int_{0}^{2\frac{\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{t-s}}}z^{4}\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}}{(t-s)^{2}z^{4}}zdz

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}s}{(t-s)^{3}},

where we have used that

Z_{0}(\rho)\leq C/\rho^{4}
and

1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4})\leq Cz^{4}
.
Therefore

\displaystyle|I_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}\frac{|p(s)|s}{(t-s)^{2}}ds\leq\|p\|_{\gamma,m}\int_{0}^{t/2}\frac{s^{1-\gamma}}{(t-s)^{2}(\log s)^{m}}ds\leq\frac{C}{t^{\gamma}(\log t)^{m}}\|p\|_{\gamma,m}.

Let us analyze
I_{2}
.
We write

\displaystyle I_{2}=I_{2,*}+I_{2,a}+I_{2,b}+I_{2,c}+I_{2,d}

where

\displaystyle I_{2,*}

\displaystyle=-16\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)(t-s)}{\lambda(s)^{4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}}{(t-s)^{2}z^{4}}zdzds

and

\displaystyle I_{2,a}

\displaystyle=\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)(t-s)}{\lambda(s)^{4}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\lambda(s)}{\sqrt{t-s}}}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\chi\Bigr(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)zdzds

\displaystyle I_{2,b}

\displaystyle=16\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)(t-s)}{\lambda(s)^{4}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\lambda(s)}{\sqrt{t-s}}}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}}{(t-s)^{2}z^{4}}\chi\Bigr(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)zdzds

\displaystyle I_{2,c}

\displaystyle=\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)(t-s)}{\lambda(s)^{4}}\int_{\frac{\lambda(s)}{\sqrt{t-s}}}^{\infty}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]\Bigl[Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)+16\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}}{(t-s)z^{4}}\Bigr]zdzds

\displaystyle I_{2,d}

\displaystyle=\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)(t-s)}{\lambda(s)^{4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\Bigl[\chi\Bigr(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)-1\Bigr]zdzds

A calculation gives that

(7.10)

\displaystyle I_{2,*}

\displaystyle=-2\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)}{t-s}ds.

Next we find a bound for
I_{2,a}
.
Using that
Z_{0}
is a bounded function and
|1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4})|\leq Cz^{4}
, we get

\displaystyle\Bigl|\int_{0}^{\frac{\lambda(s)}{\sqrt{t-s}}}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\chi\Bigr(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)zdz\Bigr|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\int_{0}^{\frac{\lambda(s)}{\sqrt{t-s}}}z^{5}dz\leq C\frac{\lambda(s)^{6}}{(t-s)^{3}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle|I_{2,a}|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{|p(s)|\lambda(s)^{2}}{(t-s)^{2}}ds\leq\frac{C}{t^{\gamma}(\log t)^{m}}\|p\|_{\gamma,m}\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{\lambda(s)^{2}}{(t-s)^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t^{\gamma}(\log t)^{m}}\|p\|_{\gamma,m}.

Using that
|1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4})|\leq Cz^{4}
, we get

\displaystyle\Bigl|\int_{0}^{\frac{\lambda(s)}{\sqrt{t-s}}}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}}{(t-s)^{2}z^{4}}\chi\Bigr(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)zdz\Bigr|

\displaystyle C\leq\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}}{(t-s)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\lambda(s)}{\sqrt{t-s}}}zdz

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\lambda(s)^{6}}{(t-s)^{3}},

and similarly as before,

\displaystyle|I_{2,b}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t^{\gamma}(\log t)^{m}}\|p\|_{\gamma,m}.

Using that

Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)=-16\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}}{(t-s)^{2}z^{4}}+O\Bigl(\frac{\lambda(s)^{6}}{(t-s)^{3}z^{6}}\Bigr),\quad\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\lambda(s)}\geq 1,

we get

\displaystyle|I_{2,c}|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)(t-s)}{\lambda(s)^{4}}\int_{\frac{\lambda(s)}{\sqrt{t-s}}}^{\infty}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]\frac{\lambda(s)^{6}}{(t-s)^{3}z^{6}}\chi\Bigr(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)zdz

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)\lambda(s)^{2}}{(t-s)^{2}}\int_{\frac{\lambda(s)}{\sqrt{t-s}}}^{\infty}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]\frac{1}{z^{5}}dz.

But
\frac{\lambda(s)}{\sqrt{t-s}}\leq 2
in the considered range of
s
, and then

\displaystyle|I_{2,c}|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{|p(s)|\lambda(s)^{2}}{(t-s)^{2}}\log\Bigl(\frac{\lambda(s)^{2}}{t-s}\Bigr)ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t^{\gamma}(\log t)^{m}}\|p\|_{\gamma,m}\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{\lambda(s)^{2}}{(t-s)^{2}}\log\Bigl(\frac{\lambda(s)^{2}}{t-s}\Bigr)ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t^{\gamma}(\log t)^{m}}\|p\|_{\gamma,m}.

Finally, for
I_{2,d}
,

\displaystyle\Bigl|\int_{0}^{\infty}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\lambda(s)}\Bigr)\Bigl[\chi\Bigr(\frac{z\sqrt{t-s}}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)-1\Bigr]zdz\Bigr|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{2\sqrt{s}/\sqrt{t-s}}^{\infty}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4}}\Bigl(1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\Bigr)\Bigr]\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}}{(t-s)^{2}z^{4}}zdz

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}}{(t-s)^{2}}\int_{2\sqrt{s}/\sqrt{t-s}}^{\infty}z^{-3}dz

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}}{(t-s)s}.

Then

\displaystyle|I_{2,d}|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{|p(s)|(t-s)}{\lambda(s)^{4}}\frac{\lambda(s)^{4}}{(t-s)s}ds\leq\frac{C}{t^{\gamma}(\log t)^{m}}\|p\|_{\gamma,m}.

Finally we estimate

\displaystyle|I_{3}|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\int_{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}^{t}\frac{p(s)}{\lambda(s)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Bigl[1-e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}\lambda^{2}}{4(t-s)}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\rho^{2}\lambda^{2}}{4(t-s)}\Bigr)\Bigr]Z_{0}(\rho)\chi\Bigr(\frac{\lambda\rho}{\sqrt{s}}\Bigl)\rho d\rho ds\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}^{t}\frac{|p(s)|}{\lambda(s)^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t^{\gamma}(\log t)^{m}}\|p\|_{\gamma,m}.

In summary, by (
7.10
) we have written

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi(x,t)dx

\displaystyle=-2\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)}{t-s}ds+I_{1}+I_{2,a}+I_{2,b}+I_{2,c}+I_{2,d}+I_{3},

and each of the expressions
I_{1}
,
I_{2,a}
,
I_{2,b}
,
I_{2,c}
,
I_{2,d}
,
I_{3}
are linear operators of
p
with the estimate

\|I_{j}[p]\|_{\gamma,m}\leq C\|p\|_{\gamma,m}.

The proof of (
7.8
) follows from the explicit expressions for the terms
I_{j}
in
R
, and similar estimates as before.

∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Suppose that
\lambda
satisfies (
7.5
) and
\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}
be given by
(
7.3
).
Then

(7.11)

\displaystyle\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}(0,t;\lambda)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lambda(t)^{2}}{4t^{2}}+O\Bigl(\frac{1}{t^{2}(\log t)^{2}}\Bigr),

as
t\to\infty
, where
O(\frac{1}{t^{2}(\log t)^{2}})
is uniform in
t_{0}
.
With
\lambda^{*}
given by (
7.7
),
if
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
satisfy

\Bigl\|\frac{\lambda_{j}}{\lambda^{*}}\Bigl\|_{L^{\infty}(t_{0}/2,\infty)}<\frac{1}{2},\quad j=1,2,

then we also have

(7.12)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{\lambda^{*}+\lambda_{1}}^{(2)}(0,t)-\varphi_{\lambda^{*}+\lambda_{2}}^{(2)}(0,t)|\leq\frac{C}{t^{2}\log t}\Bigl\|\frac{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}{\lambda^{*}}\Bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(t_{0}/2,\infty)}.

Proof.

For simplicity of notation let us write

\varphi(x,t;\lambda)=\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}(x,t)
.
Let us write the right hand side of equation (
7.3
) in the following form

\displaystyle E_{2}(x,t;\lambda)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\lambda^{2}t}U(y)\nabla_{z}z_{0}(z)\cdot z+\frac{2}{\lambda^{3}t^{1/2}}\nabla_{z}\chi_{0}(z)\cdot\nabla_{y}U(y)+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}t}\Delta_{z}\chi_{0}(z)U(y)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\lambda^{3}t^{1/2}}U(y)\nabla_{z}\chi_{0}(z)\cdot\nabla_{y}\Gamma_{0}(y),\quad y=\frac{x}{\lambda},\ z=\frac{x}{\sqrt{t}}.

To compute
\varphi(0,t;\lambda)

let us define the following approximation of it

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}(r,t)=\lambda^{2}\tilde{\varphi}(r,t),

where
\tilde{\varphi}(r,t)
solves the radial heat equation in dimension 6:

(7.13)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} {\partial}_{t}\tilde{\varphi}&={\partial}_{r}^{2}\tilde{\varphi}+\frac{5}{r}{\partial}_{r}\tilde{\varphi}+\frac{1}{t^{3}}h\Bigl(\frac{r}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr),\\
\tilde{\varphi}(r,0)&=0,\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle h(\zeta)=\frac{8}{\zeta^{4}}\left[\chi_{0}^{\prime\prime}-\frac{3}{\zeta}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(\zeta)+\frac{\zeta}{2}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(\zeta)\right].

The solution
\tilde{\varphi}(r,t)
to problem (
7.13
) can be expressed in self-similar form as

\tilde{\varphi}(r,t)=\frac{1}{t^{2}}g(\zeta),\quad\zeta=\frac{r}{\sqrt{t}}.

We find for
g
the equation

(7.14)

g^{\prime\prime}+\frac{5}{\zeta}g^{\prime}+\frac{\zeta}{2}g^{\prime}+2g+h(\zeta)=0,\quad\zeta\in(0,\infty).

Using that the function
\frac{1}{\zeta^{4}}
is in the kernel of the homogeneous equation, we find the explicit solution of (
7.14
),

g_{0}(\zeta)=-\frac{1}{\zeta^{4}}\int_{0}^{\zeta}x^{3}e^{-\frac{1}{4}x^{2}}\int_{0}^{x}h(y)e^{\frac{1}{4}y^{2}}y\,dydx.

To find the solution
\tilde{\varphi}
with suitable decay at infinity we let

(7.15)

g(\zeta)=g_{0}(\zeta)+\frac{1}{8}\bar{z}(\zeta)I,

where

\bar{z}(\zeta)=\frac{1}{\zeta^{4}}\int_{0}^{\zeta}x^{3}e^{-\frac{1}{4}x^{2}}\,dx

is a second solution of the homogeneous equation, linearly independent of
\frac{1}{\zeta^{4}}
and

\displaystyle I=\int_{0}^{\infty}x^{3}e^{-\frac{1}{4}x^{2}}\int_{0}^{x}h(y)e^{\frac{1}{4}y^{2}}y\,dydx.

We observe that

g(\zeta)=O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\zeta^{2}})\quad\hbox{as }\zeta\to+\infty,

which makes the solution (
7.15
) the only one with decay faster than
O(\zeta^{-4})
as
\zeta\to+\infty
.
An explicit calculation gives that

I=-8,

and therefore

(7.16)

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}(0,t)=-\frac{\lambda(t)^{2}}{4t^{2}}.

Then, using a barrier for the equation satisfied by
\varphi(x,t;\lambda)-\hat{\varphi}(x,t)
we get

(7.17)

\displaystyle|\varphi(x,t;\lambda)-\hat{\varphi}(x,t)|\leq C\frac{1}{t^{2}(\log t)^{2}}e^{-c\frac{|x|^{2}}{t}},

for
t\geq 2
, where
0<c<\frac{1}{4}
.
From (
7.16
) and (
7.17
) we obtain (
7.11
).

The proof of (
7.12
) is similar.

∎

Lemma 7.4
.

Suppose that
\lambda
satisfies (
7.5
) and
\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}
be given by (
7.3
).
Then

(7.18)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-2\pi\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t}-16\pi\Upsilon\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t}+O\Bigl(\frac{1}{t^{2}(\log t)^{2}}\Bigr).

where
\Upsilon
is defined in (
2.7
), that, is,

\Upsilon=\int_{0}^{\infty}(\chi_{0}(s)-1)s^{-3}ds
.

Proof.

Integrating (
7.3
)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-4\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}(0,t)-\frac{1}{2\lambda^{2}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(y)\nabla_{z}\chi_{0}(z)\cdot zdx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{E}dx.

From (
7.11
)

\displaystyle\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}(0,t)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lambda(t)^{2}}{4t^{2}}+O\Big(\frac{1}{t^{2}(\log t)^{2}}\Bigr)

and we compute

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\lambda^{2}t}U(y)\nabla_{z}\chi_{0}(z)\cdot z+\tilde{E}

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}U(y)\nabla_{z}\chi_{0}(z)\cdot z+\frac{2}{\lambda^{2}}\nabla_{x}\chi\cdot\nabla_{x}U+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\Delta_{x}\chi U-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}U\nabla\chi\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{0}

\displaystyle\quad=\Bigl[4\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t^{3}}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(s)\frac{1}{s^{3}}-64\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t^{3}}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(s)\frac{1}{s^{5}}+8\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t^{3}}(\chi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(s)+\frac{1}{s}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(s))\frac{1}{s^{4}}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+32\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t^{3}}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(s)\frac{1}{s^{5}}\Bigr]+O\Bigl(\frac{\lambda^{4}}{t^{4}}\Bigr)\chi_{\{1\leq s\leq 2\}}

\displaystyle\quad=8\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t^{3}}\frac{1}{s^{4}}\Big[\frac{s}{2}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(s)-\frac{3}{s}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(s)+\chi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(s)\Bigr]+O\Bigl(\frac{\lambda^{4}}{t^{4}}\Bigr)\chi_{\{1\leq s\leq 2\}}

where
s=\frac{r}{\sqrt{t}}
.
Then

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\lambda^{2}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(y)\nabla_{z}\chi_{0}(z)\cdot zdx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{E}dx

\displaystyle\quad=2\pi\frac{8\lambda^{2}}{t^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{s^{4}}\Big[\frac{s}{2}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(s)-\frac{3}{s}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(s)+\chi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(s)\Bigr]sds+O\Bigl(\frac{\lambda^{4}}{t^{3}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad=16\pi\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t^{2}}\Bigl[\int_{0}^{\infty}(\chi_{0}(s)-1)s^{-3}ds+\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{-3}\chi_{0}^{\prime})^{\prime}ds\Bigl]+O\Bigl(\frac{\lambda^{4}}{t^{3}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad=16\pi\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t^{2}}\Upsilon+O\Bigl(\frac{\lambda^{4}}{t^{3}}\Bigr).

Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=2\pi\frac{\lambda(t)^{2}}{t^{2}}+16\pi\Upsilon\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t^{2}}+O\Bigl(\frac{1}{t^{3}(\log t)^{2}}\Bigr)

and integrating we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-2\pi\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t}-16\pi\Upsilon\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t}+O\Bigl(\frac{1}{t^{2}(\log t)^{2}}\Bigr).

This is the desired expansion (
7.18
).
∎

As a corollary from Lemma
7.1
and Lemma
7.4
we get:

Corollary 7.1
.

Assume
\lambda
satisfies (
7.5
).
Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda}dx=-4\pi\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda(t)^{2}}\frac{\lambda\dot{\lambda}(s)}{t-s}ds-2\pi\frac{\lambda^{2}(t)}{t}-16\pi\Upsilon\frac{\lambda^{2}(t)}{t}+O\Bigl(\frac{1}{t^{2}(\log t)^{2}}\Bigr)+R[\lambda\dot{\lambda},\lambda],

where
R
is as in Lemma
7.1
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
\tilde{E}
be defined by (
3.10
).
Assume that
\lambda
satisfies (
7.5
).
Then

(7.19)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{E}|x|^{2}dx=-64\pi\Upsilon\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t}+O\Bigl(\frac{1}{t^{2}(\log t)^{2}}\Bigr).

Proof.

Similarly to the proof of Lemma
7.4
we have

\displaystyle\tilde{E}

\displaystyle=8\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t^{3}}\frac{1}{s^{4}}\Big[-\frac{3}{s}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(s)+\chi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(s)\Bigr]+O\Bigr(\frac{\lambda^{4}}{t^{4}}\Bigr)\chi_{\{1\leq s\leq 2\}},

where
r=|x|
,
s=\frac{r}{\sqrt{t}}
,
and so

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{E}|x|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=16\pi\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{s^{4}}\Big[-\frac{3}{s}\chi_{0}^{\prime}(s)+\chi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(s)\Bigr]s^{3}ds+O\Bigr(\frac{\lambda^{4}}{t^{2}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=-64\pi\frac{\lambda^{2}}{t}\Upsilon+O\Bigr(\frac{\lambda^{4}}{t^{2}}\Bigr).

This is (
7.19
).
∎

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
E
be defined by (
3.9
).
Assume that
\lambda
satisfies (
7.5
).
Then

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}E|x|^{2}dx\right|\leq\frac{C}{t\log(t)}.

Proof.

We have from (
3.9
)

\displaystyle E(\zeta,t;\lambda)=\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda^{3}}Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\zeta}{\lambda}\Bigr)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\zeta}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)+\frac{1}{2\lambda^{2}t}U\Bigl(\frac{\zeta}{\lambda}\Bigr)\nabla_{z}\chi_{0}(z)\cdot z+\tilde{E}(x,t),

and we have already computed
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{E}|x|^{2}dx
.
We have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\zeta}{\lambda}\Bigr)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\zeta}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)|\zeta|^{2}\,d\zeta

\displaystyle=2\pi\lambda^{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}Z_{0}(\rho)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda\rho}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)\rho^{3}\,d\rho

\displaystyle=O(\lambda^{4}\log(t)),

and so

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Z_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\zeta}{\lambda}\Bigr)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\zeta}{\sqrt{t}}\Bigr)|\zeta|^{2}\,d\zeta\right|\leq\frac{C}{t\log t}.

∎

7.1.
Proof of Proposition
5.1

Let

\displaystyle I[\lambda]=4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{E}(\lambda)|x|^{2}dx.

For the proof we proceed by linearization, that is we look for a function
\lambda_{0}

satisfying

|I[\lambda_{0}](t)|\leq C\frac{1}{t^{\frac{3}{2}+\sigma}},\quad t>t_{0}

with the expansion

\lambda_{0}(t)=\lambda^{*}(t)+\tilde{\lambda}_{0}(t)

where
\lambda^{*}
was defined in (
7.7
), that is,

\lambda^{*}(t)=\frac{c_{0}}{\sqrt{\log t}}

and
\tilde{\lambda}_{0}(t)
,
t>\frac{t_{0}}{2}
, is a correction. Here
c_{0}>0
is a fixed constant.

We claim that

(7.20)

\displaystyle|I[\lambda^{*}](t)|\leq C\frac{\log(\log t)}{t(\log t)^{2}},\quad t>\frac{t_{0}}{2},

with
C
independent of
t_{0}
. In the rest of the proof
C
will be a constant independent of
t_{0}
(for
t_{0}
large).

Indeed, using the decomposition (
7.1
) and the notation (
7.4
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda^{*}}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda^{*}}^{(1)}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda^{*}}^{(2)}dx

and

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda^{*}}^{(1)}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi[p^{*},\lambda^{*}]dx,\quad p^{*}=\lambda^{*}\dot{\lambda}^{*}.

By Lemma
7.1
we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi[p^{*},\lambda^{*}]dx+4\pi\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}\frac{p^{*}(s)}{t-s}ds\right|\leq C\frac{1}{t(\log t)^{2}},\quad t>\frac{t_{0}}{2}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi[p^{*},\lambda^{*}]dx+4\pi\log(t)p^{*}(t)\right|\leq C\frac{\log(\log t)}{t(\log t)^{2}},\quad t>\frac{t_{0}}{2}.

On the other hand, by Lemma
7.4
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda^{*}}^{(2)}dx

\displaystyle=-2\pi\frac{\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}{t}-16\pi\Upsilon\frac{\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}{t}+O\Bigl(\frac{1}{t(\log t)^{2}}\Bigr),

and by Lemma
7.5

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{E}(\lambda^{*})|x|^{2}dx=-64\pi\Upsilon\frac{\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}{t}+O\Bigl(\frac{1}{t^{2}(\log t)^{2}}\Bigr).

Using the explicit form of
\lambda^{*}
and the previous formulas we deduce (
7.20
).

Next let us rewrite slightly the operator

I[\lambda]
as follows. We have

\displaystyle I[\lambda]=4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi[\lambda\dot{\lambda},\lambda]dx+4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda}^{(2)}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{E}(\lambda)|x|^{2}dx.

Let us define

\displaystyle R[p,\lambda]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi[p,\lambda]dx+4\pi\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)}{t-s}ds.

This is similar to the decomposition given in Lemma
7.1
, but we have changed the interval of integration to
[\frac{t}{2},t-\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}]
.
We decompose the integral

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t-\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)}{t-s}ds

\displaystyle=\int_{t/2}^{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}\frac{p(s)}{t-s}ds+\int_{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}^{t-\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}\frac{p(s)}{t-s}ds

\displaystyle=\int_{t/2}^{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}\frac{p(s)}{t-s}ds+p(t)\int_{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}^{t-\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}\frac{1}{t-s}ds

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}^{t-\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}\frac{p(t)-p(s)}{t-s}ds

where
0<\vartheta<\frac{1}{2}
is a fixed constant.

We change variables
\mu=\lambda^{2}
, so that

\displaystyle I[\lambda]

\displaystyle=-8\pi\dot{\mu}(t)((1-\vartheta)\log(t)-2\log(\lambda^{*}(t)))-8\pi\int_{t/2}^{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}\frac{\dot{\mu}(s)}{t-s}ds

\displaystyle\quad+4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\sqrt{\mu}}^{(2)}dx+2R[\dot{\mu},\sqrt{\mu}]-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{E}(\sqrt{\mu})|x|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\quad+8\pi\int_{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}^{t-\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}\frac{\dot{\mu}(t)-\dot{\mu}(s)}{t-s}ds.

Let
\eta
be a smooth cut-off such that
\eta(t)=0
for
t<\frac{3}{4}t_{0}
,
\eta(t)=1
for
t>t_{0}
.
We define

\displaystyle\tilde{I}[\mu]

\displaystyle=-8\pi\dot{\mu}(t)((1-\vartheta)\log(t)-2\log(\lambda^{*}(t)))-8\pi\eta(t)\int_{t/2}^{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}\frac{\dot{\mu}(s)}{t-s}ds

\displaystyle\quad+4\eta(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\sqrt{\mu}}^{(2)}dx+2\eta(t)R[\dot{\mu},\sqrt{\mu}]-\eta(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{E}(\sqrt{\mu})|x|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\quad+8\pi\eta(t)\int_{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}^{t-\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}\frac{\dot{\mu}(t)-\dot{\mu}(s)}{t-s}ds

which we write

\displaystyle\tilde{I}[\mu]=\ell[\mu]+N[\mu]+R[\mu],

where

\displaystyle\ell[\mu](t)

\displaystyle=-8\pi\dot{\mu}(t)((1-\vartheta)\log(t)-2\log(\lambda^{*}(t)))-8\pi\eta(t)\int_{t/2}^{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}\frac{\dot{\mu}(s)}{t-s}ds

\displaystyle N[\mu](t)

\displaystyle=4\eta(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\sqrt{\mu}}^{(2)}dx+2\eta(t)R[\dot{\mu},\sqrt{\mu}]-\eta(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{E}(\sqrt{\mu})|x|^{2}dx

\displaystyle R[\mu](t)

\displaystyle=8\pi\eta(t)\int_{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}^{t-\lambda^{*}(t)^{2}}\frac{\dot{\mu}(t)-\dot{\mu}(s)}{t-s}ds.

Note that
I[\lambda](t)=\tilde{I}[\lambda^{2}](t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
.

Instead of finding
\lambda
such that
I[\lambda]=0
for
t>t_{0}
we are going to construct
\mu
such that

|\tilde{I}[\mu](t)|\leq\frac{C}{t^{\frac{3}{2}+\sigma}},\quad t>\frac{t_{0}}{2},

for some
\sigma>0
.

Let
\mu^{*}=(\lambda^{*})^{2}
where
\lambda^{*}
is defined in (
7.7
).
In a first step we will find
\mu_{1}
so that

(7.21)

\displaystyle\ell[\mu^{*}+\mu_{1}]+N[\mu^{*}+\mu_{1}]+R[\mu^{*}]=0,\quad t>\frac{t_{0}}{2}.

We will look for
\mu_{1}

with
\|\mu_{1}\|_{*,\gamma,m}<\infty
where, for a function
\mu_{1}\in C^{1}([\frac{t_{0}}{2},\infty))
with
\lim_{t\to\infty}\mu_{1}(t)=0
we define

\displaystyle\|\mu_{1}\|_{*,\gamma,m}=\sup_{t\geq t_{0}/2}t^{\gamma}(\log t)^{m}|\dot{\mu}_{1}(t)|=\|\dot{\mu}_{1}\|_{\gamma,m}.

Equation (
7.21
) takes the form

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-8\pi\dot{\mu}_{1}((1-\vartheta)\log(t)-2\log(\lambda^{*}(t)))-8\pi\eta(t)\int_{t/2}^{t-t^{1-\vartheta}}\frac{\dot{\mu}_{1}(s)}{t-s}ds

(7.22)

\displaystyle\quad+\eta(t)e_{1}(t)+\eta(t)F_{1}[\mu_{1}](t),\quad t>\frac{t_{0}}{2},

where

e_{1}(t)=\tilde{I}[\mu^{*}]

and
F_{1}
is an operator with the following properties:

(7.23)

\displaystyle\|F_{1}[\tilde{\mu}_{1}]\|_{\gamma,m}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\tilde{\mu}_{1}\|_{*,\gamma,m},

(7.24)

\displaystyle\|F_{1}[\tilde{\mu}_{1}]-F_{1}[\tilde{\mu}_{2}]\|_{\gamma,m}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\tilde{\mu}_{1}-\tilde{\mu}_{2}\|_{*,\gamma,m},

for
\tilde{\mu}_{j}
satisfying
\|\tilde{\mu}_{j}\|_{*,\gamma,m}\leq 1
, with
0<\gamma<2
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
, where
\|\ \|_{\gamma,m}
is defined in (
7.6
).
From (
7.20
) we find

|e_{1}(t)|\leq C\frac{\log(\log t)}{t(\log t)^{2}},\quad t>\frac{t_{0}}{2}.

Now we apply the contraction mapping principle to the equation (
7.22
) written in the form

(7.25)

\displaystyle\dot{\mu}_{1}
