\displaystyle\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}\Bigl(\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr).

Analogously,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi^{\perp}(T_{2})|y|^{2}\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{y}{R(T_{2})}\Bigr)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq Cf(T_{2})R(T_{2})^{2}\Bigl(\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr)

(10.77)

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}\Bigl(\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr).

Integrating by parts

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}B[\phi(s)]|y|^{2}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R(s)})dyds\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\frac{1}{s\log s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\phi(y,s)||y|^{2}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R(s)})dyds

(10.78)

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\frac{1}{s\log s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\phi(y,s)||y|^{2}|\nabla\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R(s)})|dyds.

Let’s estimate, using (
10.73
)

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\frac{1}{s\log s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\phi(y,s)||y|^{2}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R(s)})dyds

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\frac{1}{s}f(s)R(s)^{2}ds\Bigl(\frac{1}{R(\tau_{0})}\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}\Bigl(\frac{1}{R(\tau_{0})}\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr).

The second term in (
10.78
) is even smaller, and we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}B[\phi(s)]|y|^{2}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R(s)})dyds\right|

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}\Bigl(\frac{1}{R(\tau_{0})}\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}

(10.79)

\displaystyle\quad+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr).

From (
10.73
) we also get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\frac{R^{\prime}(s)}{R(s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi(s)|y|^{2}\nabla\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R(s)})\cdot\frac{y}{R(s)}dyds\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\frac{R^{\prime}(s)}{R(s)}f(s)R(s)^{2}ds\Bigl(\frac{1}{R(\tau_{0})}\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr)

(10.80)

\displaystyle\quad\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}\Bigl(\frac{1}{R(\tau_{0})}\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr).

Next we look at

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}L[\phi]|y|^{2}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R})dy

\displaystyle=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U\nabla g\cdot y\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R})dy-\frac{1}{R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U|y|^{2}\nabla g\cdot\nabla\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R})dy

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}gZ_{0}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R})dy+\frac{4}{R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}gUy\cdot\nabla\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R})dy

(10.81)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g|y|^{2}\nabla U\cdot\nabla\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R})dy+\frac{1}{R^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g|y|^{2}U\Delta\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R})dy.

We have
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}gZ_{0}=0
by Lemma
9.2
and therefore, using (
10.72
), we find that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}gZ_{0}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R(\tau)})dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B_{R(\tau)}(0)}gZ_{0}dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq f(\tau)\Bigl(\frac{1}{R(\tau_{0})}\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr).

The remaining terms in (
10.81
) are estimated using (
10.70
) or (
10.72
)
and we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}L[\phi]|y|^{2}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R})dy\right|\leq Cf(\tau)\Bigl(\frac{1}{R(\tau_{0})}\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr).

Therefore

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}L[\phi(s)]|y|^{2}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R(s)})dyds\right|

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}(\log\tau)^{q}\Bigl(\frac{1}{R(\tau_{0})}\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}

(10.82)

\displaystyle\quad+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr).

Finally

(10.83)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h|y|^{2}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R(s)})dyds\right|

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}(\log\tau)^{q}\|h\|_{**}.

From (
10.75
), (
10.76
), (
10.77
), (
10.79
), (
10.80
), (
10.82
), and (
10.83
) we get

(10.84)

\displaystyle|a(\tau)|\log\tau\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}(\log\tau)^{q}\Bigl(\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr).

Assuming
\tau_{0}
large, we deduce that

(10.85)

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}\leq\frac{C}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\Bigl(\|h\|_{**}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr).

Note that
a(\tau_{0})
and
c_{1}
are related. Indeed,
the initial condition is
\phi_{0}=c_{1}\tilde{Z}_{0}=\phi_{0}^{\perp}+\frac{a(\tau_{0})}{2}Z_{0}
with

\displaystyle a(\tau_{0})=\frac{c_{1}}{8\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{Z}_{0}\Gamma_{0},

by (
10.7
).
We note that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{Z}_{0}\Gamma_{0}=16\pi+O(\frac{\log\tau_{0}}{\tau_{0}})
.
So by (
10.85
)

\displaystyle|c_{1}|\leq C|a(\tau_{0})|\leq C\frac{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\|h\|_{**}+C\frac{1}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}|c_{1}|.

For
\tau_{0}
large, we deduce that

(10.86)

\displaystyle|c_{1}|\leq C|a(\tau_{0})|\leq C\frac{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\|h\|_{**}.

This proves (
10.17
).
Replacing this in (
10.85
) we get

(10.87)

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}\leq\frac{C}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\|h\|_{**},

which proves (
10.15
).
Combining (
10.69
), (
10.86
) and (
10.87
) we obtain (
10.16
).

Finally, we also obtain from (
10.73
)

(10.88)

\displaystyle|\phi(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\frac{1}{1+\rho^{4}}\|h\|_{**}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
10.2
.

The proof is a slight modification of the one of Lemma
10.1
.
Using the same notation as in that proof, integrating (
10.74
) from
\tau
to
T_{2}>\tau
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi(T_{2})|y|^{2}\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{y}{R(T_{2})}\Bigr)dy

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi(\tau)|y|^{2}\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{y}{R(\tau)}\Bigr)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(L[\phi(s)]+h)|y|^{2}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R(s)})dyds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}B[\phi(s)]|y|^{2}\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R})dyds

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\tau}^{T_{2}}\frac{R^{\prime}(s)}{R(s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi(s)|y|^{2}\nabla\chi_{0}(\frac{y}{R(s)})\cdot\frac{y}{R(s)}dyds,

Similarly to (
10.84
) we obtain

\displaystyle|a(\tau)|\log\tau

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}(\log\tau)^{q}\Bigl(\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr)

(10.89)

\displaystyle\quad+C|a(T_{2})|\log(T_{2}).

The assumption
\frac{a}{fR^{2}}\in L^{\infty}(\tau_{0},\infty)
implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}a(\tau)\log\tau=0.

Letting
T_{2}\to\infty
in (
10.89
) we obtain

\displaystyle|a(\tau)|\log\tau\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}(\log\tau)^{q}\Bigl(\|h\|_{**}+\Bigl\|\frac{a}{R^{2}f}\Bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\tau_{0},\infty)}+|c_{1}|\frac{1}{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\Bigr).

Then the same argument as in Lemma
10.1
gives the estimates for
a
,
\omega
and
c_{1}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
10.3
.

Assume to the contrary that there is some
T_{2}>\tau_{0}
such that

a_{Z}(T_{2})=0.

Then by Lemma
10.1

a_{Z}(\tau)=0
for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},T_{2}
]. But by (
10.7
)

\displaystyle a(\tau_{0})=\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Gamma_{0}\tilde{Z}_{0}=2+O(\frac{\log\tau_{0}}{\tau_{0}})\not=0,

which is a contradiction.
∎

Proof of Lemma
10.4
.

We let
T_{n}
be a sequence such that
T_{n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
.
Let
\bar{\phi}
be the solution to (
10.1
) with initial condition equal to
0
. This solution exists but for the moment we don’t have any control of its asymptotic behavior as
\tau\to\infty
.
Let
\bar{\phi}^{\perp}
,
\bar{a}(\tau)
be the decomposition (
10.6
) of
\bar{\phi}
. Let
Z_{B}^{\perp}
,
a_{Z}(\tau)
be the decomposition (
10.6
) of
Z_{B}
.
Using Lemma
10.3
there is
c_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\bar{a}(T_{n})+c_{n}a_{Z}(T_{n})=0.

Let us define

\displaystyle\phi_{n}=\bar{\phi}+c_{n}Z_{B},

and let

\displaystyle\phi_{n}=\phi_{n}^{\perp}+\frac{a_{n}}{2}Z_{0}

be the decomposition (
10.6
) of
\phi_{n}
.
Then by Lemma
10.1
we have

\displaystyle|a_{n}(\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\|h\|_{**},\quad\tau\in[\tau_{0},T_{n}]

\displaystyle|\omega_{n}(\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\|h\|_{**},\quad\tau\in[\tau_{0},T_{n}]

\displaystyle|c_{n}|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\|h\|_{**}.

Moreover, we also have the uniform estimate

\displaystyle|\phi_{n}(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\frac{1}{1+\rho^{4}}\|h\|_{**}

for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},T_{n}]
from (
10.88
).

By using standard parabolic estimates, passing to a subsequence we may assume that
c_{n}\to c_{1}
and
\phi_{n}\to\phi
locally uniformly in space-time, and that
\phi
is a solution of (
10.10
) for some
c_{1}
such that

\displaystyle|c_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{f(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\|h\|_{**}.

Moreover
\phi
satisfies

\displaystyle|\phi(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\frac{1}{1+\rho^{4}}\|h\|_{**}

and writing the decomposition (
10.6
) as
\phi=\phi^{\perp}+\frac{a}{2}Z_{0}
we have

\displaystyle|a(\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\|h\|_{**}.

We also get

\displaystyle|\omega_{n}(\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\|h\|_{**},

where
\omega
is defined in (
10.14
).

The uniqueness of
c_{1}
is a consequence of Lemma
10.2
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
10.1
.

We have already constructed
\phi
and
c_{1}
in Lemma
10.4
,
we have the uniqueness of
\phi
and the estimates for
a
and
c_{1}
in Lemma
10.2
.

We only need to prove the estimate for
\phi^{\perp}
stated in (
10.11
).
By the construction of
\phi
in Lemma
10.4
and
(
10.71
), (
10.86
) and (
10.87
), we get

(10.90)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(g^{\perp})^{2}U\leq Cf(\tau)^{2}R(\tau)^{2}\|h\|_{**}^{2},\quad\tau>\tau_{0}.

We claim that from this inequality we have

\displaystyle U|g^{\perp}(y,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{2}}\|h\|_{**}.,\quad\tau>\tau_{0}.

The proof of this estimate is similar to that of (
10.58
) in Lemma
10.8
.

Indeed, we define

\displaystyle g_{0}^{\perp}=Ug^{\perp}

and obtain the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}g_{0}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot\Bigl(U\nabla\Bigl(\frac{g_{0}^{\perp}}{U}\Bigr)\Bigr)-U(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\cdot\Bigl(U\nabla\Bigl(\frac{g_{0}^{\perp}}{U}\Bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+h-U(-\Delta)^{-1}h

\displaystyle\quad+B[g_{0}^{\perp}]-U(-\Delta)^{-1}B[g_{0}^{\perp}]+B[U\psi[g_{0}^{\perp}]-U(-\Delta)^{-1}B[U\psi[g_{0}^{\perp}]

(10.91)

\displaystyle\quad+a^{\prime}(\tau)U+\frac{a}{2}B[Z_{0}]-\frac{a}{2}U(-\Delta)^{-1}B[Z_{0}].

Here the notation
\psi[g_{0}^{\perp}]
is the one introduced in the proof of Lemma
10.7
in (
10.39
).

To get an estimate for the solution we need an estimate for
a^{\prime}(\tau)
.
Since
g^{\perp}=g+a
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Ug^{\perp}=0
we have

\displaystyle a(\tau)=-\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Ug(\tau)dy=-\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g_{0}(\tau)dy.

But integrating (
10.38
) we find

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g_{0}(\tau)dy

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(-\Delta)^{-1}\Bigl(\nabla\cdot(U\nabla\frac{g_{0}}{U})\Bigr)dy-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(-\Delta)^{-1}hdy

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(-\Delta)^{-1}(B[g_{0}+U\psi[g_{0}]])dy,

which gives the expression

\displaystyle a^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(-\Delta)^{-1}\Bigl(\nabla\cdot(U\nabla\frac{g_{0}}{U})\Bigr)dy+\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(-\Delta)^{-1}hdy

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(-\Delta)^{-1}(B[g_{0}+U\psi[g_{0}]])dy.

We claim that

(10.92)

\displaystyle|a^{\prime}(\tau)|\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)\|h\|_{**}.

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(-\Delta)^{-1}\Bigl(\nabla\cdot(U\nabla\frac{g_{0}}{U})\Bigr)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Gamma_{0}\nabla\cdot(U\nabla g^{\perp})dy=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla U\cdot\nabla g^{\perp}dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Delta Ug^{\perp}.

Then, by (
10.90
)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(-\Delta)^{-1}\Bigl(\nabla\cdot(U\nabla\frac{g_{0}}{U})\Bigr)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(g^{\perp})^{2}U\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)\|h\|_{**}.

We also have, for the case of the operator (
10.2
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(-\Delta)^{-1}(B[g_{0}])\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Gamma_{0}B[g_{0}]=\zeta(\tau)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Gamma_{0}\nabla\cdot(yg_{0})dy

\displaystyle=-\zeta(\tau)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla\Gamma_{0}\cdot yUgdy

But by construction and (
10.70
), (
10.86
) and (
10.87
), we get

(10.93)

\displaystyle\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g^{2}U\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq\frac{C}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}\|h\|_{**}.

so, using (
10.93
)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U(-\Delta)^{-1}(B[g_{0}])\,dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\tau\log\tau}\Bigr(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Ug^{2}\Bigl)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\tau\log\tau}\frac{1}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}\|h\|_{**}

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\tau)\|h\|_{**}

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)\|h\|_{**}.

The last term is estimated similarly and we get (
10.92
).

Repeating the argument in of Lemma
10.7
we obtain from (
10.90
)

\displaystyle|g_{0}^{\perp}(y,\tau)|\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)\|h\|_{**}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{2}}.

An argument similar to Lemma
10.9
gives

\displaystyle|\phi^{\perp}(\rho,\tau)|\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{2}}\|h\|_{**}.

∎

We have an estimate for
\phi^{\perp}
stronger than (
10.11
) under a stricter assumption on
\nu
.

Lemma 10.10
.

Let us assume that
1<\nu<\frac{3}{2}
.
Under the same assumption of Proposition
10.1

let
\phi=\phi^{\perp}+\frac{a}{2}Z_{0}
be the solution of (
10.10
).
Then

\displaystyle|\phi^{\perp}(y,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq CR(\tau)f(\tau)\|h\|_{**}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{2}}&|y|\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau}{|y|^{4}}&|y|\geq\sqrt{\tau},\end{cases}

Proof.

We write (
10.91
) as

(10.94)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}g_{0}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\Delta g_{0}^{\perp}-\nabla g_{0}^{\perp}\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{0}+2Ug_{0}^{\perp}+B[g_{0}^{\perp}]+\tilde{h}_{1}

where

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{1}

\displaystyle=-U(-\Delta)^{-1}(\nabla\cdot(g_{0}^{\perp}\nabla\Gamma_{0}))

\displaystyle\quad-U(-\Delta)^{-1}B[g_{0}^{\perp}]+B[U\psi[g_{0}^{\perp}]]-U(-\Delta)^{-1}B[U\psi[g_{0}^{\perp}]]

\displaystyle\quad+a^{\prime}(\tau)U+\frac{a}{2}B[Z_{0}]-\frac{a}{2}U(-\Delta)^{-1}B[Z_{0}]

\displaystyle\quad+h-U(-\Delta)^{-1}h.

Then, similarly to (
10.59
), we have

\displaystyle|\tilde{h}_{1}(y,\tau)|\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)\|h\|_{**}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{4}}.

Let

\displaystyle\bar{g}^{\perp}(\rho,\tau)

\displaystyle=f(\tau)R(\tau)\tilde{g}_{3}(\rho)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\rho}{\delta\sqrt{\tau}}\Bigr)+A_{1}\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)}{\tau}\frac{1}{(1+\rho/\sqrt{\tau})^{4}}+A_{2}\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)}{\tau}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{4\tau}}

where
-\Delta_{6}\tilde{g}_{3}=\frac{1}{1+\rho^{4}}
with
\tilde{g}_{2}(\rho)\to 0
as
\rho\to\infty
.
If
\nu<\frac{3}{2}
, for appropriate positive constants
\delta
,
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
, and
C
, the function
C\|h\|_{**}\bar{g}^{\perp}
is supersolution to (
10.94
) in
\{(y,\tau)|\tau>\tau_{0},\ |y|>R_{0}\}
.
We deduce that

\displaystyle|g_{0}^{\perp}(y,\tau)|\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)\|h\|_{**}\frac{\min(1,\frac{\tau}{|y|^{2}})}{(1+|y|)^{2}}.

An argument similar to Lemma
10.9
gives

\displaystyle|\phi^{\perp}(\rho,\tau)|\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)\|h\|_{**}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{1+|y|^{2}}&|y|\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau}{|y|^{4}}&|y|\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

∎

Proof of Proposition
8.1
.

By Proposition
10.1
there is
c_{1}
such that the solution
\phi
to
(
10.10
) has the properties stated in Proposition
10.1
.
We recall that by (
10.88
)
\phi
satisfies

(10.95)

\displaystyle|\phi(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\frac{1}{1+\rho^{4}}\|h\|_{**}.

We will construct a barrier to estimate
\phi
for
|y|\geq R_{0}
, where
R_{0}
is a large constant.
We consider the equation (
10.10
) in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B_{R_{0}}(0)
written in the form

(10.96)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\phi=\Delta\phi-4\nabla\Gamma_{0}\nabla\phi+2U\phi+B[\phi]+\bar{h},

where

\bar{h}=-\nabla U\nabla\psi+h.

Since
\psi=(-\Delta)^{-1}\phi
, from (
10.95
) we get

\displaystyle|\nabla\psi(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\frac{1}{1+\rho^{3}}\|h\|_{**}.

This gives

(10.97)

\displaystyle|\nabla U\cdot\nabla\psi|\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\frac{1}{1+\rho^{8}}\|h\|_{**}.

By (
10.97
) and the definition of the norm
\|h\|_{**}
,

|\bar{h}(y,\tau)|\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{6+\sigma}}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau^{\epsilon/2}}{\rho^{\epsilon}}\Bigr)\|h\|_{**},

where we have used that
\sigma+\epsilon<2
.
Let
\tilde{g}_{2}
be defined by (
10.61
) and let

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}(\rho,\tau)

\displaystyle=f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}\tilde{g}_{2}(\rho)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\rho}{\delta\sqrt{\tau}}\Bigr)+A_{1}\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{\tau^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho/\sqrt{\tau})^{6+\sigma+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\quad+A_{2}\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{\tau^{2}}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{4\tau}}.

Then for suitable positive constants
\delta
,
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
, and
C
, the function
C(\log\tau_{0})^{q-1}\|h\|_{**}\bar{\phi}
is a supersolution to (
10.96
) in
\{(y,\tau)|\tau>\tau_{0},\ |y|>R_{0}\}
.
For this we need
\nu<2
.
Moreover

|\phi(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\bar{\phi}(\rho,\tau)(\log\tau_{0})^{q-1}\|h\|_{**}
at
\rho=R_{0}
by (
10.95
).
By the maximum principle

\displaystyle|\phi(y,\tau)|\leq C\bar{\phi}(y,\tau)(\log\tau_{0})^{q-1}\|h\|_{**},\quad|y|>R_{0}.

This gives the explicit bound

\displaystyle|\phi(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\frac{f(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho^{4})}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau^{1/2}}{\rho}\Bigr)^{2+\sigma+\epsilon}\|h\|_{**}

∎

We include here some results that will be useful later.
Let

\displaystyle\hat{Z}_{0}=L[\tilde{Z}_{0}].

Lemma 10.11
.

The function
\hat{Z}_{0}
satisfies

(10.98)

\displaystyle|\hat{Z}_{0}(\rho)|\leq C\frac{1}{\tau_{0}(1+\rho)^{4}}

and is supported on
\rho\leq 2\tau_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
\psi=(-\Delta)^{-1}\tilde{Z}_{0}
and
g=\frac{\tilde{Z}_{0}}{U}-\psi
. By (
10.9
) and using that
Z_{0}=Uz_{0}
,
z_{0}
defined in (
9.2
),

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=\frac{(Z_{0}-m_{Z_{0}}U)\chi}{U}-\psi=z_{0}\chi-m_{Z_{0}}\chi-\psi,

where
\chi(\rho)=\chi_{0}(\frac{\rho}{\sqrt{\tau_{0}}})
.
Note that
\tilde{Z}_{0}
has mass zero and support in
B_{2\sqrt{\tau_{0}}}
. It follows that
\psi
has also support contained in
B_{2\sqrt{\tau_{0}}}
and then
g
has support contained in
B_{2\sqrt{\tau_{0}}}
.
Therefore
\hat{Z}_{0}=L[\tilde{Z}_{0}]=\nabla\cdot(U\nabla g)
has also support contained in
B_{2\sqrt{\tau_{0}}}
.

To get an estimate for
\hat{Z}_{0}
let us write

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle=(-\Delta)^{-1}(Z_{0}-m_{Z_{0}}U)\chi)=(-\Delta)^{-1}Z_{0}+\psi_{1},

where

\displaystyle\psi_{1}=(-\Delta)^{-1}(Z_{0}(\chi-1)-m_{Z_{0}}U\chi).

Since
\Delta z_{0}+Uz_{0}=0
and
\lim_{\rho\to\infty}z_{0}(\rho)=-2
we have

(-\Delta)^{-1}Z_{0}=z_{0}+2
. So

\displaystyle\psi=z_{0}+2+\psi_{1}

Hence

\displaystyle g=z_{0}(\chi-1)-2-m_{Z_{0}}\chi-\psi_{1}

and so

\displaystyle\hat{Z}_{0}

\displaystyle=L[\tilde{Z}_{0}]=\nabla\cdot(U\nabla g)

(10.99)

\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot(U(\nabla z_{0}(\chi-1)+z_{0}\nabla\chi-m_{Z_{0}}\nabla\chi-\nabla\psi_{1})).

Using radial symmetry and
m_{Z_{0}}=O(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}})
we get

\displaystyle|\nabla\psi_{1}(\rho)|\leq C\frac{1}{\tau_{0}(1+\rho)}.

From this and (
10.99
) we get (
10.98
).
∎

Consider the initial value problem

(10.100)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\phi_{1}&=L[\phi_{1}]+B[\phi_{1}]\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty),\\
\phi_{1}(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=\hat{Z}_{0}\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

Lemma 10.12
.

Let
0<\gamma<2
.
Let
1<\nu_{0}<\frac{7}{4}

\displaystyle f_{0}(\tau)=\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu_{0}}}.

and let
R(\tau)
be as in (
10.12
).
Then the solution
\phi_{1}
of (
10.100
) satisfies

\displaystyle|\phi_{1}(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\frac{f_{0}(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{\tau_{0}f_{0}(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho^{4})}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau^{1/2}}{\rho}\Bigr)^{2+\gamma}.

Proof.

A suitable modification in the proof of Proposition
10.1
gives the following result. Consider

(10.101)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\phi&=L[\phi]+B[\phi]\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty),\\
\phi(\cdot,t_{0})&=\phi_{0}+c_{1}\tilde{Z}_{0}\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2},\end{aligned}\right.

Then there is
C>0
such that for any
\tau_{0}
sufficiently large the following holds. Suppose that
\phi_{0}
is a radial function with zero mass in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, supported in
B_{2\sqrt{\tau_{0}}}(0)
, and such that

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}(\rho)|\leq M\frac{1}{1+\rho^{4}}.

Then there exists
c_{1}
such that the solution
\phi
of (
10.101
) satisfies

\displaystyle|\phi(\rho,\tau)|\leq CM\frac{f_{0}(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{f_{0}(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho^{4})}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau^{1/2}}{\rho}\Bigr)^{2+\gamma}.

Moreover
c_{1}
is a linear function of
\phi_{0}
and satisfies

\displaystyle|c_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq CM\frac{1}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}.

Let us apply this statement to
\phi_{0}=L[\tilde{Z}_{0}]
, which is radial, with mass zero, support in
B_{2\sqrt{\tau_{0}}}(0)
, and satisfies

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}(\rho)|\leq\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\frac{1}{1+\rho^{4}},

by Lemma
10.11
.
Then there exists
c_{1}
such that the solution
\tilde{\phi}
to (
10.101
) with
\phi_{0}=L[\tilde{Z}_{0}]
satisfies

(10.102)

\displaystyle|\tilde{\phi}(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\frac{f_{0}(\tau)R(\tau)^{2}}{\tau_{0}f_{0}(\tau_{0})R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho^{4})}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau^{1/2}}{\rho}\Bigr)^{2+\gamma}.

We claim that
c_{1}=0
. To prove this, we multiply (
10.101
) by
|y|^{2}
and integrate on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty)
.
Let’s work with

\displaystyle B[\phi]=\zeta(\tau)\nabla\cdot(y\phi).

The case of the operator (
10.3
) is similar.
Then we get

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{\phi}(y,\tau)|y|^{2}dy=-2\zeta(\tau)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{\phi}(y,\tau)|y|^{2}dy,

because
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}L[\phi]|y|^{2}dy=0
, see Remark
9.2
.
Integrating

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{\phi}(y,\tau)|y|^{2}dy

\displaystyle=e^{-2\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\zeta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{\phi}(y,\tau_{0})|y|^{2}dy=c_{1}e^{-2\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\zeta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{Z}_{0}(y)|y|^{2}dy,

because
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}L[\tilde{Z}_{0}]|y|^{2}dy=0
.
Using the asymptotic expansion of
\zeta
one gets

\displaystyle e^{-2\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\zeta}\to\infty,\quad\text{as }\tau\to\infty.

But the bound (
10.102
) implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{\phi}(y,\tau)|y|^{2}dy=0.

This only can happen if
c_{1}=0
.

We deduce that
\phi_{1}
defined in (
10.100
) coincides with
\tilde{\phi}
, and then (
10.102
) holds for
\phi_{1}
.
∎

11.
Linear estimate with second moment (radial)

We will prove in this section Proposition
8.2
in the radial case
h(\rho,\tau)
.

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
0<\sigma<1
,
\epsilon>0
with
\sigma+\epsilon<2
and
1<\nu<\min(1+\frac{\epsilon}{2},3-\frac{\sigma}{2},\frac{5}{4})
.
Let
0<q<1
.
Then there is
C
such that for
\tau_{0}
large the following holds.
Suppose that
h
satisfies
\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}<\infty
and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h(y,\tau)dy

\displaystyle=0,\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h(y,\tau)|y|^{2}dy=0.

Then the solution
\phi(y,\tau)
of problem (
8.9
)
satisfies

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{\nu-\frac{1}{2},m+\frac{q}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}.

To describe the idea of the proof more easily let us consider for a moment the equation (
8.9
) without
B
:

(11.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\phi&=L[\phi]+h(y,t)\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
\phi(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2},\end{aligned}\right.

The idea is to formally apply a suitable left inverse
L^{-1}
of
L
to (
11.1
) (to be defined later on in Lemma
11.1
).
If we call
\Phi=L^{-1}\phi
,
H=L^{-1}h
, then we would like to solve

(11.2)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\Phi&=L[\Phi]+H(y,t)\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
\Phi(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

In order to get good properties of
H
, in this step we have already used that
h
satisfies the second moment condition. At this point we would like to apply Proposition
10.1
, which gives a decomposition

\displaystyle\Phi=\Phi^{\perp}+\frac{a(\tau)}{2}Z_{0}.

Note that
\Phi^{\perp}
decays in time like
1/\tau^{\nu-1/2}
and so
\phi=L\Phi
also decays in time like
1/\tau^{\nu-1/2}
, which is better than the estimate provided by Proposition
8.1
.
It turns out that
H
decays in space like
1/\rho^{4+\sigma}
so we can’t apply directly Proposition
10.1
to (
11.2
). What we do is
concentrate

H
by solving first a nicer problem. We write
\Phi=\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{2}
where
\Phi_{1}
is asked to solve

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\Phi_{1}&=L_{0}[\Phi_{1}]+H(y,t)\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
\Phi_{1}(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

where

(11.3)

\displaystyle L_{0}[\phi]=\nabla\cdot\Bigl(U\nabla\Bigl(\frac{\phi}{U}\Bigr)\Bigr)=\Delta\phi-\nabla\phi\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{0}+U\phi.

Lemma
11.2
below deals with
\Phi_{1}
.
Then the problem for
\Phi_{2}
becomes

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\Phi_{2}&=L_{0}[\Phi_{2}]+L[\Phi_{1}]-L_{0}[\Phi_{1}]\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
\Phi_{1}(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

It turns out that the right hand side in this equation has better spatial decay and we can apply Proposition
10.1
.

In the next lemmas we give some preliminary results, and the proof of Proposition
11.1
is given at the end of this section.

We define the inverse of
L
that we use.
For
h:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
define
\|h\|_{\tau,6+\sigma,\epsilon}

as the smallest
K
such that

\displaystyle|h(y)|\leq\frac{K}{(1+|y|)^{6+\sigma}}\begin{cases}1&|y|\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{\epsilon/2}}{|y|^{\epsilon}}&|y|\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

which depends on
\tau
, treated as parameter here,
\sigma
, and
\varepsilon
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
\sigma,\epsilon>0
.
Let
h=h(\rho)
be radial and satisfy

\|h\|_{\tau,6+\sigma,\epsilon}<\infty
and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}hdy=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h|y|^{2}dy=0.

Then there exists
H
radially symmetric such that
L[H]=h
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and satisfies

(11.4)

\displaystyle\|H\|_{\tau,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq C\|h\|_{\tau,6+\sigma,\epsilon}

Moreover,
H
defines a linear operator of
h
and satisfies

(11.5)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Hdy=0.

Proof.

Write the equation
L[H]=h
as

\nabla\cdot(U\nabla g)=h

where
g=\frac{H}{U}-(-\Delta)^{-1}H
.
We choose
g
as

g(\rho)=-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\frac{1}{rU(r)}\int_{0}^{r}h(s)sdsdr.

Using that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h=0
we check that

|g(\rho)|\leq C\|h\|_{\tau,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{\sigma}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{\epsilon/2}}{\rho^{\sigma+\epsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}\end{cases}

Now we solve Liouville’s equation

-\Delta\psi-U\psi=Ug\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2},\quad\psi(\rho)\to 0\quad\text{as }\rho\to\infty,

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h|y|^{2}dy=0
we check that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}gZ_{0}dy=0.

Then we can use the variations of parameter formula, and get

|\psi(\rho)|\leq C\|h\|_{\tau,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{2+\sigma}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{\epsilon/2}}{\rho^{2+\sigma+\varepsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}\end{cases}

Then define
H=U(g+\psi)
, which is the desired solution, and note that it satisfies (
11.4
).
Property (
11.5
) follows from
H=-\Delta\psi
and the decay of
\psi
.
∎

To take into account the operator
B
we
define

\displaystyle\Lambda[\phi]=y\cdot\nabla\phi,

and compute

(11.6)

\displaystyle\Lambda\circ L[\Phi]-L\circ\Lambda[\Phi]

\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot(\Phi Uy)-2L[\Phi]-\nabla\cdot((y\cdot\nabla U+2U)\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\Phi).

Indeed, write
\Psi=(-\Delta)^{-1}\Phi
. Then

(11.7)

\displaystyle L\Phi=\Delta\Phi-\nabla\Gamma_{0}\cdot\nabla\Phi-\nabla U\cdot\nabla\Psi+2U\Phi.

By direct computation

(11.8)

\displaystyle\Lambda\Delta\Phi

\displaystyle=\Delta\Lambda\Phi-2\Delta\Phi

(11.9)

\displaystyle\Lambda(\nabla\Gamma_{0}\cdot\nabla\Phi)

\displaystyle=\nabla(\Lambda\Gamma_{0})\cdot\nabla\Phi+\nabla\Gamma_{0}\cdot\nabla(\Lambda\Phi)-2\nabla\Gamma_{0}\cdot\nabla\Phi

(11.10)

\displaystyle\Lambda(\nabla U\cdot\nabla\Psi)

\displaystyle=\nabla(\Lambda U)\cdot\nabla\Psi+\nabla U\cdot\nabla(\Lambda\Psi)-2\nabla U\cdot\nabla\Psi.

But
-\Delta\Psi=\Phi
and therefore

\displaystyle-\Delta(\Lambda\Psi)+2\Delta\Psi=\Lambda\Phi.

Applying
(-\Delta)^{-1}
gives

\displaystyle\Lambda\Psi=(-\Delta)^{-1}(\Lambda\Phi)+2\Psi.

Substituting this into (
11.10
) we obtain

\displaystyle\Lambda(\nabla U\cdot\nabla\Psi)

\displaystyle=\nabla(\Lambda U)\cdot\nabla\Psi+\nabla U\cdot\nabla[(-\Delta)^{-1}(\Lambda\Phi)+2\Psi]-2\nabla U\cdot\nabla\Psi

(11.11)

\displaystyle=\nabla(\Lambda U)\cdot\nabla\Psi+\nabla U\cdot\nabla[(-\Delta)^{-1}(\Lambda\Phi)].

Combining (
11.7
), (
11.8
), (
11.9
), (
11.11
) we find that

\displaystyle\Lambda L\Phi=L\Lambda\Phi-2L\Phi+4U\Phi-2\nabla U\cdot\nabla\Psi-\nabla(\Lambda\Gamma_{0})\cdot\nabla\Phi-\nabla(\Lambda U)\cdot\nabla\Psi+2\Lambda(U)\Phi.

But

\displaystyle-2\nabla U\cdot\nabla\Psi-\nabla(\Lambda U)\cdot\nabla\Psi

\displaystyle=-\nabla Z_{0}\cdot\nabla\Psi

\displaystyle=-\nabla\cdot(Z_{0}\nabla\Psi)-Z_{0}\Phi,

so that

\displaystyle\Lambda L\Phi=L\Lambda\Phi-2L\Phi+4U\Phi-\nabla(\Lambda\Gamma_{0})\cdot\nabla\Phi+2\Lambda(U)\Phi-\nabla\cdot(Z_{0}\nabla\Psi)-Z_{0}\Phi.

Using that

\displaystyle 2\Lambda(U)\Phi-Z_{0}\Phi

\displaystyle=-2U\Phi+\Lambda(U)\Phi

we then obtain

\displaystyle\Lambda L\Phi=L\Lambda\Phi-2L\Phi+2U\Phi-\nabla(\Lambda\Gamma_{0})\cdot\nabla\Phi+\Lambda(U)\Phi-\nabla\cdot(Z_{0}\nabla\Psi)

Let’s consider the terms
2U\Phi-\nabla(\Lambda\Gamma_{0})\cdot\nabla\Phi+\Lambda(U)\Phi
.
Noting that
\nabla(\Lambda\Gamma_{0})=\nabla(y\cdot\nabla\Gamma+2)=\nabla z_{0}
and that
Z_{0}=2U+\Lambda(U)
,
we can write

\displaystyle 2U\Phi-\nabla(\Lambda\Gamma_{0})\cdot\nabla\Phi+\Lambda(U)\Phi

\displaystyle=2U\Phi-\nabla z_{0}\cdot\nabla\Phi+\Lambda(U)\Phi

\displaystyle=Z_{0}\Phi-\nabla\cdot(\nabla z_{0}\Phi)+\Delta z_{0}\Phi.

But
\Delta z_{0}+Z_{0}=0
, so

\displaystyle\Lambda L\Phi=L\Lambda\Phi-2L\Phi-\nabla\cdot(\nabla z_{0}\Phi)-\nabla\cdot(Z_{0}\nabla\Psi).

We can again write
\nabla z_{0}=\nabla(y\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{0})
and using the radial symmetry of the functions
\Gamma_{0}
,
z_{0}
and the notation
\rho=|y|

\displaystyle\nabla z_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{y}{\rho}\partial_{\rho}z_{0}=\frac{y}{\rho}\partial_{\rho}(\rho\partial_{\rho}\Gamma_{0})=y\Delta\Gamma_{0}=-yU.

Then

\displaystyle\Lambda L\Phi=L\Lambda\Phi-2L\Phi+\nabla\cdot(yU\Phi)-\nabla\cdot(Z_{0}\nabla\Psi).

This proves (
11.6
).

Formula (
11.6
) leads us to consider the following equation for
\Phi=L^{-1}[\phi]
:

(11.12)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\Phi&=L[\Phi]+\tilde{B}[\Phi]+\zeta_{1}(\tau)A[\Phi]+H\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
\Phi(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=0\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle A[\Phi]=L^{-1}[\nabla\cdot(\Phi Uy)-\nabla\cdot(Z_{0}\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\Phi)],

Z_{0}(y)=2U(y)+y\cdot\nabla_{y}U(y)
,
and
\tilde{B}
has the same form as
B
:

\tilde{B}[\Phi]=\tilde{\zeta}_{1}(\tau)y\cdot\nabla\Phi+\tilde{\zeta}_{2}(\tau)\Phi

with
\tilde{\zeta}_{1}(\tau)
,
\tilde{\zeta}_{2}(\tau)
satisfying

(11.13)

\displaystyle|\tilde{\zeta}_{i}(\tau)|\leq\frac{C}{\tau\log\tau}\quad\text{for all }\tau>\tau_{0}.

and
\zeta_{1}
satisfies the same restriction, that is, (
10.21
).

The next lemma allows us to reduce to an equation like (
11.12
) but with a right hand side with more spatial decay.

Lemma 11.2
.

Let
\sigma>0
,
\epsilon>0
and
1<\nu<\min(1+\frac{\epsilon}{2},3-\frac{\sigma}{2})
.
Let
H(y,\tau)
be radial in
y
and satisfy

(11.14)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}H(\cdot,\tau)=0

and
\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}<\infty
.
Then there exists
H_{1}
and
\Phi_{1}
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\Phi_{1}&=L[\Phi_{1}]+\tilde{B}[\Phi_{1}]+H-H_{1},\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
\Phi_{1}(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

Moreover
\Phi_{1}
and
H_{1}
are linear operators of
H
and satisfy

(11.15)

\displaystyle|\Phi_{1}(\rho,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{2+\sigma}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{1+\epsilon/2}}{(1+\rho)^{4+\sigma+\epsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

(11.16)

\displaystyle|H_{1}(\rho,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{6+\sigma}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{\epsilon/2}}{(1+\rho)^{6+\sigma+\epsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases},

(11.17)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Phi_{1}dy

\displaystyle=0

(11.18)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}H_{1}(\cdot,\tau)

\displaystyle=0.

Proof.

Write the operator
L
as

L[\phi]=L_{0}[\phi]-\nabla\cdot(U\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\phi)

where
L_{0}
is defined in (
11.3
).
Consider the problem

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\Phi_{1}&=L_{0}[\Phi_{1}]+\tilde{B}[\Phi_{1}]+H,\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty),\\
\Phi_{1}(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

The idea is to formally apply
L_{0}^{-1}
to this equation.
Similarly to the proof of (
11.6
) we compute

\displaystyle\Lambda\circ L_{0}[\Phi]-L_{0}\circ\Lambda[\Phi]

\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot(\Phi Uy)-2L_{0}[\Phi].

This leads us to consider the problem

(11.19)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\tilde{\Phi}&=L_{0}[\tilde{\Phi}]+B_{1}[\tilde{\Phi}]+\tilde{H},\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty),\\
\tilde{\Phi}(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2},\end{aligned}\right.

where
\tilde{H}
is a radial function satisfying

L_{0}[\tilde{H}]=H\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}

and

B_{1}[\tilde{\Phi}]=\hat{\zeta}_{1}(\tau)y\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Phi}+\hat{\zeta}_{2}(\tau)\tilde{\Phi}

with

(11.20)

\displaystyle\hat{\zeta}_{1}(\tau)=\tilde{\zeta}_{1}(\tau)=O\Bigl(\frac{1}{\tau\log\tau}\Bigr),\quad\hat{\zeta}_{2}(\tau)=\tilde{\zeta}_{2}(\tau)-2\tilde{\zeta}_{1}(\tau)=O\Bigl(\frac{1}{\tau\log\tau}\Bigr),

by (
11.13
).

We claim that there is a choice of
\tilde{H}
, which defines a linear operator of
H
, and satisfies

(11.21)

\displaystyle|\tilde{H}|+(1+\rho)|\nabla\tilde{H}|\leq C\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{2+\sigma}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{\epsilon/2}}{(1+\rho)^{2+\sigma+\epsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

Indeed, the equation
L_{0}[\tilde{H}]=H
for radial functions has the form

\partial_{\rho}\Bigl(\rho U\partial_{\rho}\Bigl(\frac{\tilde{H}}{U}\Bigr)\Bigr)=\rho H.

We select the solution

\displaystyle\tilde{H}(\rho,\tau)=U(\rho)\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{1}{rU(r)}\int_{0}^{r}H(s,\tau)sdsdr.

Using (
11.14
) we get (
11.21
).

Instead of (
11.19
) we consider

(11.22)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\tilde{\Phi}_{1}&=\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{1}-\nabla\Gamma_{0}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Phi}_{1}+B_{1}[\tilde{\Phi}_{1}]+\tilde{H},\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty),\\
\tilde{\Phi}_{1}(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2},\end{aligned}\right.

We then have the following estimate for
\tilde{\Phi}_{1}
:

(11.23)

\displaystyle|\tilde{\Phi}_{1}|\leq\frac{C}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{\sigma}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{1+\epsilon/2}}{(1+\rho)^{2+\sigma+\epsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

For the proof of this we construct a barrier. First we find a solution to

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi_{1}-\nabla\Gamma_{0}\cdot\nabla\phi_{1}+\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{2+\sigma}}

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2},

\displaystyle\phi_{1}(\rho)

\displaystyle\to 0\quad\text{as }\rho\to\infty.

The equation may be integrated explicitly, noting that

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi-\nabla\Gamma_{0}\cdot\nabla\phi=\phi_{\rho\rho}+\Bigl(\frac{1}{\rho}+\frac{4\rho}{1+\rho^{2}}\Bigr)\phi_{\rho}

and that the constants are in the kernel of this operator.
We then have

\displaystyle\phi_{1}(\rho)=\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r(1+r^{2})^{2}}\int_{0}^{r}\frac{1}{(1+s)^{2+\sigma}}s(1+s^{2})^{2}dsdr

and this implies

\displaystyle|\phi_{1}(\rho)|+(1+\rho)|\phi_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+\rho)^{\sigma}}

Let

\displaystyle\chi(\rho,\tau)=\chi_{0}(\frac{\rho}{\delta\sqrt{\tau}}),

where
\chi_{0}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
\chi_{0}(s)=1
for
s\leq 1
and
\chi_{0}(s)=0
for
s\geq 2
.
Define
\tilde{\phi}_{1}=\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\phi_{1}\chi
. We have

\displaystyle(\partial_{\tau}-\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}+\nabla\Gamma_{0}\cdot\nabla)\tilde{\phi}_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}(1+\rho)^{2+\sigma}}\chi-\frac{C_{1}}{\tau^{\nu+\sigma/2+1}(\log\tau)^{m}}\chi_{\{\delta\sqrt{\tau}\leq\rho\leq 2\delta\sqrt{\tau}\}},

for some
C_{1}>0
,
\delta>0
(assuming
\tau_{0}
large).
Now consider

\displaystyle\phi_{2}(\rho,\tau)=\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu+\sigma/2}(\log\tau)^{m}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho/\sqrt{\tau})^{2+\sigma+\epsilon}},\qquad\phi_{3}(\rho,\tau)=\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu+\sigma/2}(\log\tau)^{m}}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{4\tau}}.

A computation, using (
11.20
), shows that

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}=A_{1}\tilde{\phi}_{1}+A_{2}\phi_{2}+A_{3}\phi_{3}

satisfies

\displaystyle(\partial_{\tau}-\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}+\nabla\Gamma_{0}\cdot\nabla+B_{1})\bar{\phi}\geq\frac{c}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{2+\sigma}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{\epsilon/2}}{(1+\rho)^{2+\sigma+\epsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

for some
c>0
.
This step needs
\nu-1<\frac{\epsilon}{2}
and
\nu+\frac{\sigma}{2}<3
.
By comparison, we find that
\tilde{\Phi}_{1}
satisfies (
11.23
).

The solution
\tilde{\Phi}_{1}
of (
11.22
) satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\tilde{\Phi}_{1}

\displaystyle=L_{0}[\tilde{\Phi}_{1}]-U\tilde{\Phi}_{1}+B_{1}[\tilde{\Phi}_{1}]+\tilde{H}

Applying
L_{0}
to this equation we find that

\Phi_{1}=L_{0}[\tilde{\Phi}_{1}]

satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\Phi_{1}

\displaystyle=L[\Phi_{1}]+\tilde{B}[\Phi_{1}]+H-H_{1}

with

(11.24)

\displaystyle H_{1}=-\nabla\cdot(U\nabla\Psi_{1})+L_{0}[U\tilde{\Phi}_{1}]+\tilde{\zeta}_{1}\nabla\cdot(\tilde{\Phi}_{1}Uy),\quad\Psi_{1}=(-\Delta)^{-1}\Phi_{1}.

Let us verify that
\Phi_{1}
and
H_{1}
satisfy the conditions stated in
(
11.15
), (
11.16
), (
11.18
).
Indeed, from standard parabolic estimates and (
11.23
) we have

(11.25)

\displaystyle|\nabla\tilde{\Phi}_{1}|\leq\frac{C}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{1+\sigma}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{1+\epsilon/2}}{(1+\rho)^{3+\sigma+\epsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

Differentiating in
y_{j}
,
j=1,2
the equation (
11.22
) and using standard parabolic estimates, together with (
11.21
), (
11.25
) we obtain

(11.26)

\displaystyle|D^{2}\tilde{\Phi}_{1}|\leq\frac{C}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{2+\sigma}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{1+\epsilon/2}}{(1+\rho)^{4+\sigma+\epsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

The definition
\Phi_{1}=L_{0}[\tilde{\Phi}_{1}]
and the estimates (
11.23
), (
11.25
), (
11.26
) give the estimate (
11.15
).

We compute

\displaystyle H_{1}=-\nabla U\cdot\nabla\Psi_{1}+U\Phi_{1}+\nabla U\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Phi}_{1}+U\Delta\tilde{\Phi}_{1}+\tilde{\zeta}_{1}\nabla\cdot(\tilde{\Phi}_{1}Uy).

Note that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Phi_{1}(\cdot,\tau)=0
. So,
by a direct radial computation of
\Psi_{1}=(-\Delta)^{-1}\Phi_{1}
and (
11.15
) we obtain

\displaystyle|\nabla\Psi_{1}(\rho,\tau)|\leq\frac{C}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{1+\sigma}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{1+\epsilon/2}}{(1+\rho)^{3+\sigma+\epsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

This estimate and the ones already obtained for
\tilde{\Phi}_{1}
(
11.25
), (
11.26
) and for
\Phi_{1}
(
11.15
) yield

\displaystyle|H_{1}(\rho,\tau)|\leq\frac{C}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{6+\sigma}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{\epsilon/2}}{(1+\rho)^{6+\sigma+\epsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases},

which is the desired estimate (
11.16
).

Finally, the zero mass condition (
11.18
) follows from the form of
H_{1}
(
11.24
) and its decay. The mass condition for
\Phi_{1}
(
11.17
) follows from
\Phi_{1}=L_{0}[\tilde{\Phi}_{1}]
and the decay of
\tilde{\Phi}_{1}
(
11.23
) and (
11.25
).
∎

Next we would like to obtain a result similar to Proposition
10.1
for the problem (
11.12
). In order to simplify this step, we will modify this equation by allowing a parameter in the initial condition. This technical obstruction will be removed in the proof of Proposition
11.1
.
Thus we consider

(11.27)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\Phi&=L[\Phi]+\tilde{B}[\Phi]+\zeta_{1}(\tau)A[\Phi]+H\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
\Phi(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=c_{1}\tilde{Z}_{0},\end{aligned}\right.

where
\tilde{Z}_{0}
is defined in (
10.9
).

The next result allows us to say that if in equation (
11.27
) the right hand side has fast decay, then we can decompose the solution similarly as in Proposition
10.1
. This result is an extension of that proposition to an equation that has the extra operator
A
in it, which is treated as a perturbation.

Lemma 11.3
.

Let
0<\sigma<1
,
\epsilon>0
,
\sigma+\epsilon<2
,

1<\nu<\min(1+\frac{\epsilon}{2},3-\frac{\sigma}{2},\frac{3}{2})
.
Let
0<q<1
.
Then there is
C>0
such that for
\tau_{0}
sufficiently large and for
H
radially symmetric with
\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}<\infty
and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}H(y,\tau)dy=0\quad\text{for all }\tau>\tau_{0}

the solution
\Phi
to (
11.27
) can be decomposed as
\Phi=\Phi_{0}+\frac{a(\tau)}{2}Z_{0}
with the estimates

\displaystyle|\Phi_{0}(\rho,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\min\Bigl(\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{2}},\frac{\tau}{|y|^{4}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle|a(\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-1}(\log\tau)^{m+q}}.

Moreover
\Phi_{0}
and
a
are linear operators of
H
.

Proof of Lemma
11.3
.

We will treat the operator
A
as a perturbation and therefore consider

(11.28)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\Phi&=L[\Phi]+\tilde{B}[\Phi]+H\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
\Phi(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=c_{1}\tilde{Z}_{0}.\end{aligned}\right.

Let
\Phi_{1}
,
H_{1}
be the functions constructed in Lemma
11.2
.
Setting
\Phi=\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{2}
, (
11.28
) is equivalent to the following equation for

\Phi_{2}

(11.29)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\Phi_{2}&=L[\Phi_{2}]+\tilde{B}[\Phi_{2}]+H_{1},\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty),\\
\Phi_{2}(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=c_{1}\tilde{Z}_{0}\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

We now apply Proposition
10.1
to (
11.29
). We have that

\|H_{1}\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}<\infty
by (
11.16
),
H_{1}
is radial
and satisfies the zero mass condition (
11.18
).
By Proposition
10.1
and Lemma
10.10

there exists
c_{1}
such that the solution
\Phi_{2}
of (
11.29
) satisfies

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}(y,\tau)=\Phi_{2}^{\perp}(y,\tau)+\frac{a(\tau)}{2}Z_{0}(y),

with the estimates

(11.30)

\displaystyle|\Phi_{2}^{\perp}(y,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\|H_{1}\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{2}}&|y|\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{|y|^{4}}&|y|\geq\sqrt{\tau},\end{cases}

(11.31)

\displaystyle|a(\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\|H_{1}\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}}{\tau^{\nu-1}(\log\tau)^{m+q}}.

(We are ignoring the factor
\frac{1}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1-q}}
in the estimate of
a(\tau)
.)
We also know that
c_{1}
is a linear function of
H_{1}
and satisfies

\displaystyle|c_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\|H_{1}\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}}{\tau_{0}^{\nu-1}(\log\tau_{0})^{m+1}}.

Combining (
11.15
) and (
11.30
) we conclude that
\Phi
, the solution to (
11.28
),
can be decomposed as

\Phi=\Phi_{0}+\frac{a(\tau)}{2}Z_{0}

where
\Phi_{0}(y,\tau)=\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{2}^{\perp}
is radial and satisfies

\displaystyle|\Phi_{0}(y,\tau)|\leq C\frac{\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\min\Bigl(\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{2}},\frac{\tau}{|y|^{4}}\Bigr)

and
a(\tau)
satisfies, combining (
11.16
) and (
11.31
),

\displaystyle|a(\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-1}(\log\tau)^{m+q}}\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}.

We summarize the previous finding as follows. Given
H
radial satisfying
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}H(\cdot,\tau)=0
for
\tau>\tau_{0}
and
\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}<\infty
, let us denote
T_{0}(H)=\Phi_{0}=\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{2}^{\perp}
and
T_{a}(H)=a(\tau)
so that the solution
\Phi
of (
11.28
), is
\Phi=\Phi_{0}+\frac{a(\tau)}{2}Z_{0}=T_{0}[H]+\frac{1}{2}T_{a}[H]Z_{0}
. Then
T_{0}
,
T_{a}
are linear and have the estimates

(11.32)

\displaystyle\|T_{0}[H]\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}

(11.33)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}[H]\|_{a}

\displaystyle\leq C\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon},

where

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{0}\|_{0}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\tau>\tau_{0},\,y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}\frac{1}{\min\Bigl(\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{2}},\frac{\tau}{|y|^{4}}\Bigr)}|\Phi_{0}(y,\tau)|

\displaystyle\|a\|_{a}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\tau>\tau_{0}}\tau^{\nu-1}(\log\tau)^{m+q}|a(\tau)|.

Moreover
c_{1}
is a linear function of
H
and satisfies

\displaystyle|c_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}}{\tau_{0}^{\nu-1}(\log\tau_{0})^{m+1}}.

We will apply these estimates to treat problem (
11.27
), which can be written as the fixed point problem

\displaystyle\Phi_{0}=T_{0}[H+\zeta_{1}A[\Phi_{0}+aZ_{0}]]

\displaystyle a=T_{a}[H+\zeta_{1}A[\Phi_{0}+aZ_{0}]]

By (
11.32
) and (
11.33
)

\displaystyle\|T_{0}[\zeta_{1}A[\Phi_{0}+aZ_{0}]]\|_{0}+\|T_{a}[\zeta_{1}A[\Phi_{0}+aZ_{0}]]\|_{a}\leq C\|\zeta_{1}A[\Phi_{0}+aZ_{0}]\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}.

We claim that

(11.34)

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{1}A[\Phi_{0}]\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq C\tau_{0}^{-\vartheta}\|\Phi_{0}\|_{0},

for some
\vartheta>0
,
where
C
is independent of
\tau_{0}
, and

(11.35)

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{1}A[aZ_{0}]\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1+q}}\|a\|_{a}.

Assume for the moment that (
11.34
), (
11.35
) hold. The we see that

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{0}\|_{0}+\|a\|_{a}\leq\frac{C}{(\log\tau_{0})^{1+q}}(\|\Phi_{0}\|_{0}+\|a\|_{a})+C\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon}.

For
\tau_{0}
large this gives

\|\Phi_{0}\|_{0}+\|a\|_{a}\leq C\|H\|_{\nu,m,4+\sigma,\epsilon},

which is the desired result.

For the proof of estimates (
11.34
), (
11.35
) we will need the following property.
If
\Phi
satisfies
|\Phi(y)|\leq\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{2+\kappa}}
for some
\kappa>0
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Phi dy=0
, then

(11.36)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla\cdot[\Phi Uy-Z_{0}\nabla\Psi]|y|^{2}dy=0,\quad\Psi=(-\Delta)^{-1}\Phi.

Indeed,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla\cdot(\Phi Uy)|y|^{2}dy

\displaystyle=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Phi U|y|^{2}dy=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Delta\Psi U|y|^{2}dy

\displaystyle=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla\Psi\cdot\nabla(U|y|^{2})dy

\displaystyle=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla\Psi\cdot yZ_{0}dy

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla\cdot(Z_{0}\nabla\Psi)|y|^{2}dy

\displaystyle=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Z_{0}\nabla\Psi\cdot ydy.

To prove (
11.34
), let us write
\Psi_{0}=(-\Delta)^{-1}\Phi_{0}
.
Then

\displaystyle A[\Phi_{0}]=L^{-1}[\nabla\cdot(\Phi_{0}Uy-Z_{0}\nabla\Psi_{0})].

Using the definition of
L^{-1}
given in Lemma
11.1
we have
that

L^{-1}[\nabla\cdot(\Phi Uy)-\nabla\cdot(Z_{0}\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\Phi)]=Ug+U\psi

where

(11.37)

\displaystyle g(\rho,\tau)=-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\Bigl[\Phi_{0}(s,\tau)s-\frac{Z_{0}(s)}{U(s)}\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{0}(s,\tau)\Bigr]ds,

and
\psi
is the decaying solution
to the Liouville equation

\displaystyle-\Delta\psi-U\psi=Ug.

From the definition
\Psi_{0}=(-\Delta)^{-1}\Phi_{0}
and using that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Phi_{0}dy=0
we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{0}(\rho,\tau)=\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\Phi_{0}(s,\tau)sds

which gives the estimate

\displaystyle|\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{0}(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\|\Phi_{0}\|_{0}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\begin{cases}\frac{\log(\frac{2\sqrt{\tau}}{1+\rho})}{1+\rho}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau},\\
\frac{\tau}{\rho^{3}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

Then formula (
11.37
) gives

\displaystyle|g(\rho,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi_{0}\|_{0}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\begin{cases}\log^{2}(\frac{2\sqrt{\tau}}{1+\rho})&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau},\\
\frac{\tau}{\rho^{2}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi_{0}\|_{0}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}-2}}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau}{\rho^{2}}\Bigr).

We note that by (
11.36
) we have
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Ugz_{0}dy=0
.
Then,
\psi
has the estimate

\displaystyle|\psi(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\|\Phi_{0}\|_{0}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}-2}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{2}}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau}{\rho^{2}}\Bigr).

It follows that
A[\Phi_{0}]=Ug+U\psi
satisfies

\displaystyle|A[\Phi_{0}](\rho,\tau)|\leq C\|\Phi_{0}\|_{0}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}-2}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{4}}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau}{\rho^{2}}\Bigr).

From this inequality we obtain (
11.34
).

The proof of (
11.35
) is similar.
This time
A[aZ_{0}]=Ug_{1}+U\psi_{1}
where

\displaystyle g_{1}(\rho,\tau)=-a(\tau)\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\Bigl[Z_{0}(s)s-\frac{Z_{0}(s)}{U(s)}z_{0}^{\prime}(s)\Bigr]ds,

and
\psi_{1}
is the radial decaying solution to

\displaystyle-\Delta\psi_{1}-U\psi_{1}=Ug_{1}.

We then obtain that

\displaystyle|A[aZ_{0}](\rho,\tau)|\leq C\|a\|_{a}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-1}(\log\tau)^{m+q}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{6}}.

From this estimate we deduce (
11.35
).
∎

Before proving Proposition
11.1
as stated, we obtain a version of it for the problem

(11.38)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\phi&=L[\phi]+B[\phi]+h(y,\tau)\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty),\\
\phi(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=c_{1}\hat{Z}_{0}\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2},\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle\hat{Z}_{0}=L[\tilde{Z}_{0}].

Lemma 11.4
.

Let
0<\sigma<1
,
\epsilon>0
,
\sigma+\epsilon<2

and
1<\nu<\min(1+\frac{\epsilon}{2},3-\frac{\sigma}{2},\frac{3}{2})
.
Let
0<q<1
.
Then there is
C
such that for
\tau_{0}
large the following holds.
Suppose that
h
is radially symmetric, satisfies
\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}<\infty
and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h(y,\tau)dy

\displaystyle=0,\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h(y,\tau)|y|^{2}dy=0,\quad\tau>\tau_{0}.

Then there exist
c_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and a solution
\phi(y,\tau)
of problem
(
11.38
)
that define linear operators of
h
and
satisfy

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{\nu-\frac{1}{2},m+\frac{q-1}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}.

\displaystyle|c_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\tau_{0}^{\nu-1}(\log\tau_{0})^{m+1}}\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}.

Proof.

Consider equation (
11.27
), where
H
is the function constructed in Lemma
11.1
.
By Lemma
11.3
, there is
c_{1}
such that the solution
\Phi
of (
11.27
)
can be decomposed as
\Phi=\Phi_{0}+\frac{a(\tau)}{2}Z_{0}
,
where
\Phi_{0}
and
a
satisfy the estimates stated in that proposition. In combination with (
11.4
) we find

(11.39)

\displaystyle|\Phi_{0}(\rho,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\min\Bigl(\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{2}},\frac{\tau}{|y|^{4}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle|a(\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-1}(\log\tau)^{m+q}}.

(11.40)

\displaystyle|c_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\tau_{0}^{\nu-1}(\log\tau_{0})^{m+1}}\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}.

Moreover
\Phi_{0}
,
a
,
c_{1}
are linear operators of
H
.

From standard parabolic estimates and (
11.39
) we obtain

(11.41)

\displaystyle|\nabla\Phi_{0}(\rho,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\min\Bigl(\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{3}},\frac{\tau}{|y|^{5}}\Bigr).

We consider the equation for
\Phi_{0}=\Phi-\frac{a(\tau)}{2}Z_{0}
, obtained from (
11.27
), and
differentiate with respect to
y_{j}
,
j=1,2
. Using standard parabolic estimates, together with (
11.39
), (
11.41
), and the bound for
a^{\prime}(\tau)
in (
10.92
), we obtain

(11.42)

\displaystyle|D^{2}\Phi_{0}(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\min\Bigl(\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{4}},\frac{\tau}{|y|^{6}}\Bigr).

Let us define
\phi=L[\Phi]
. Then
\phi
satisfies (
11.38
) because
L[Z_{0}]=0
and thanks to (
11.39
), (
11.41
), (
11.42
) we find

(11.43)

\displaystyle|\phi(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\min\Bigl(\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{4}},\frac{\tau}{|y|^{6}}\Bigr).

In the rest of the proof we show that

\displaystyle|\phi(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{4}}\begin{cases}1&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{1+\sigma/2+\epsilon/2}}{\rho^{2+\sigma+\epsilon}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

For this we consider the equation (
11.38
) written in the form

(11.44)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\phi=\Delta\phi-\nabla\Gamma_{0}\nabla\phi+2U\phi+B[\phi]+\bar{h},

where

\bar{h}=-\nabla U\nabla\psi+h.

Using (
11.43
) and the radial formula for
\psi=(-\Delta)^{-1}\phi
, we get

\displaystyle|\nabla\psi(y,\tau)|\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{3}}&\rho\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau}{\rho^{5}}&\rho\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

This estimate and the definition of the norm
\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}
, give

|\bar{h}(y,\tau)|\leq C\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{6+\sigma}}&|y|\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{1}{\tau^{3+\sigma/2}(\frac{|y|}{\sqrt{\tau}})^{6+\sigma+\epsilon}}&|y|\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

We now construct a barrier very similar to the proof of Proposition
8.1

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}(\rho,\tau)

\displaystyle=A_{1}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\tilde{g}_{2}(\rho)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\rho}{\sqrt{\tau}}\Bigr)+A_{2}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu+\frac{3}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho/\sqrt{\tau})^{6+\sigma+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\quad+A_{3}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu+\frac{3}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m+\frac{q}{2}}}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}}{4\tau}},

where
\tilde{g}_{2}
is the function (
10.61
).
We consider (
11.44
) in
\{\,(y,\tau)\ |\ \tau>\tau_{0},\ |y|>R_{0}\,\}
where
R_{0}>0
is a large constant.
For suitable constants
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
,
A_{3}
,
C
the function
C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\bar{\phi}
is a supersolution. This computation requires
\nu<\frac{3}{2}
.

Moreover
\phi(y,\tau)\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\bar{\phi}(y,\tau)
at
|y|=R_{0}
. The initial conditions also compare well. Indeed, by Lemma
10.11
and (
11.40
)

\displaystyle|\phi(\rho,\tau_{0})|

\displaystyle=c_{1}|\hat{Z}_{0}(\rho)|\leq C\frac{1}{\tau_{0}^{\nu-1}(\log\tau_{0})^{m+1}}\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\frac{1}{1+\rho^{6}},

and this is supported on
\rho\leq 2\sqrt{\tau}_{0}
, so

\displaystyle|\phi(\rho,\tau_{0})|\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}\bar{\phi}(y,\tau).

By the maximum principle

\displaystyle|\phi(y,\tau)|\leq C\bar{\phi}(y,\tau)\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon},\quad|y|>R_{0}.

This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
11.1
.

Let
\hat{\phi}
,
c_{1}
be the solution to (
11.38
) constructed in Lemma
11.4
.
Let
\phi_{1}
be the solution to (
10.100
).
By Lemma
10.12

\phi_{1}
satisfies

(11.45)

\displaystyle|\phi_{1}(\rho,\tau)|\leq C\frac{\tau_{0}^{\nu_{0}-1}R(\tau)^{2}}{\tau^{\nu_{0}}R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho^{4})}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau^{1/2}}{\rho}\Bigr)^{2+\sigma+\epsilon},

where
1<\nu_{0}<\frac{7}{4}
.
Then the solution
\phi
to (
8.9
) that we construct is given by

\displaystyle\phi=\hat{\phi}-c_{1}\phi_{1}.

To get the desired estimate on
\phi
we need to estimate
|c_{1}\phi_{1}|
.
Let
f
be given by (
10.13
).
By (
11.40
) and (
11.45
)

\displaystyle|c_{1}\phi_{1}(\rho,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\tau_{0}^{\nu-1}(\log\tau_{0})^{m+1}}\frac{\tau_{0}^{\nu_{0}-1}R(\tau)^{2}}{\tau^{\nu_{0}}R(\tau_{0})^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+\rho^{4})}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau^{1/2}}{\rho}\Bigr)^{2+\sigma+\epsilon}\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\log\tau_{0}R(\tau_{0})}f(\tau)R(\tau)\frac{1}{(1+\rho^{4})}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau^{1/2}}{\rho}\Bigr)^{2+\sigma+\epsilon}\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\tau)R(\tau)\frac{1}{(1+\rho^{4})}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau^{1/2}}{\rho}\Bigr)^{2+\sigma+\epsilon}\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}

provided
\frac{1}{2}+\nu-\nu_{0}<0
. But
\nu_{0}
can be taken close to
\frac{7}{4}
, so we obtain the result by assuming
\nu<\frac{5}{4}
in addition to the other constraints needed in Lemma
11.4
, namely
1<\nu<\min(1+\frac{\epsilon}{2},3-\frac{\sigma}{2},\frac{3}{2})
.
∎

12.
Linear estimate with second moment (general)

A convenient property of problem (
8.3
) is that it can be split into Fourier modes. If we decompose

(12.1)

\displaystyle h(y,\tau)

\displaystyle=h_{0}(|y|,\tau)+h_{1}(y,\tau),\quad h_{0}(\rho,\tau)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}h(\rho e^{i\theta},\tau)d\theta

(12.2)

\displaystyle\phi(y,\tau)

\displaystyle=\phi_{0}(|y|,\tau)+\phi_{1}(y,\tau),\quad\phi_{0}(\rho,\tau)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\phi(\rho e^{i\theta},\tau)d\theta,

then
\phi
solves (
8.3
) if and only if
\phi_{i}
solves (
8.3
) where
h
is replaced with
h_{i}
, for
i=0,1
. If
h=h_{1}
we say that
h

has no radial mode
.

For the proof Proposition
8.2
in the general case we will consider in a first step the equation (
8.3
) but without the operator
B
, namely,

(12.3)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\phi&=L[\phi]+h,\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty),\\
\phi(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2},\end{aligned}\right.

for functions with no radial mode, as explained at the beginning of Section
11
.
Later on, we will consider equation (
8.3
) for functions with no radial mode, where we will treat the operator
B[\phi]
as a perturbation term that can be assimilated to the right hand side.

The main step in the proof is the following estimate, valid when the functions involved have no radial mode.

Proposition 12.1
.

Let
0<\sigma<1
,
0<\epsilon<2
,
0<\nu<\min(1+\frac{\epsilon}{2},\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\sigma}{2})
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then there is a
C>0
such that for any
\tau_{0}
sufficiently large the following holds.
Suppose that
h(y,\tau)
has no radial mode and satisfies
\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}<\infty
,

(12.4)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h(y,\tau)y_{j}dy=0\quad\text{for all }\tau>\tau_{0},\quad j=1,2.

Then the solution
\phi(y,\tau)
of (
12.3
) satisfies

(12.5)

\displaystyle|\phi(y,\tau)|\leq C\frac{\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{3+\sigma}},&|y|\leq\sqrt{\tau}.\\
\frac{\tau^{1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}}{|y|^{5+\sigma+\epsilon}},&|y|\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Since
h(y,\tau)
has no radial mode, all functions involved in the proof have also this property.
We use the notation from §
9.2
, particular
g=\frac{\phi}{U}-(-\Delta)^{-1}\phi
,
g^{\perp}=g-a
with
a(\tau)\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g^{\perp}(y,\tau)Udy=0.

But

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g(y,\tau)Udy=0

because
g
has no radial mode, so that
a(\tau)=0
,
g^{\perp}=g
,
\phi^{\perp}=\phi
.
Then the proof proceeds as the proof of Proposition
10.1
with some simplifications, since there is no need to estimate
a
.

We write (
12.3
) as

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\phi=\nabla\cdot(U\nabla g^{\perp})+h,\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty).

We multiply this equation by
g
and integrate in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Let
R>0
be a large fixed constant and let

\displaystyle f(\tau)=\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}.

Let
T_{2}>\tau_{0}
and let

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{\infty,T_{2}}=\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},T_{2}]}|\varphi(\tau)|.

The following estimates are valid for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},T_{2}]
.
As in the proof of Proposition
10.1
we get

(12.6)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g^{2}U\leq Cf(\tau)^{2}R^{2}\Bigl(\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}^{2}+\Bigl\|\frac{\omega}{fR}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}^{2}\Bigr),

where

\omega(\tau)=\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B_{R}}g(\tau)^{2}U\Bigr)^{1/2}.

Similarly as in Lemma
10.8
, from (
12.6
) we get

(12.7)

\displaystyle|Ug(y,\tau)|\leq Cf(\tau)R\Bigl(\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}+\Bigl\|\frac{\omega}{fR}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}\Bigr)\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{3+\sigma}}.

The proof is presented below.
We use this to estimate

\displaystyle\omega(\tau)=\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B_{R}}g^{2}U\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq Cf(\tau)R^{1-\sigma}\Bigl(\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}+\Bigl\|\frac{\omega}{fR}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}\Bigr),

which implies

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(\tau)}{f(\tau)R}\leq CR^{-\sigma}\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}+CR^{-\sigma}\Bigl\|\frac{\omega}{fR}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}.

We deduce that

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\frac{\omega}{fR}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}\leq CR^{-\sigma}\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon},

by choosing
R
as a large constant.

Now we let
T_{2}\to\infty
and find

(12.8)

\displaystyle\omega(\tau)\leq Cf(\tau)R\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}^{2},\quad\tau>\tau_{0}.

The inequalities that follow hold for
\tau>\tau_{0}
.

Combining (
12.8
) with (
12.6
) we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g^{2}U\leq Cf(\tau)^{2}R^{2}\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}^{2},\quad\tau>\tau_{0}.

and using (
12.7
) we also get

\displaystyle|Ug(y,\tau)|\leq Cf(\tau)R\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{3+\sigma}}.

Let
\psi=(-\Delta)^{-1}\phi
so that
\phi=Ug+U\psi
. Using Lemma
9.1
and the previous estimate we obtain

(12.9)

\displaystyle|\psi(y,\tau)|+(1+|y|)|\nabla\psi(y,\tau)|\leq C\frac{R}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{1+\sigma}}\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}.

We consider the equation (
12.3
) in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B_{R}(0)
written in the form

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\phi=\Delta\phi-\nabla\Gamma_{0}\nabla\phi+2U\phi+\bar{h},

where

\bar{h}=-\nabla U\nabla\psi+h.

By (
12.9
) and the definition of the norm
\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}
,

|\bar{h}(y,\tau)|\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{5+\sigma}}\begin{cases}1&|y|\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{\epsilon/2}}{|y|^{\epsilon}}&|y|\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

Here we are using
\epsilon<2
.
Using barriers as in the proof of Lemma
10.8
we get

\displaystyle|\phi(y,\tau)|\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{3+\sigma}}\begin{cases}1&|y|\leq\sqrt{\tau}\\
\frac{\tau^{1+\epsilon/2}}{|y|^{2+\epsilon}}&|y|\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

(For this we need
\nu<1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}
,
\nu+\frac{\sigma}{2}<\frac{3}{2}
.)
This proves (
12.5
).

∎

Proof of (
12.7
).

We define

g_{0}=Ug,

which satisfies the equation

(12.10)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}g_{0}

\displaystyle=\Delta g_{0}-\nabla g_{0}\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{0}+2Ug_{0}+\tilde{h}

where

\displaystyle\tilde{h}=Uv+h-U(-\Delta)^{-1}h

and

\displaystyle v:=(-\Delta)^{-1}(\nabla\cdot(g_{0}\nabla\Gamma_{0})).

As in the proof of Lemma
10.7
we obtain

(12.11)

\displaystyle|g_{0}(y,\tau)|\leq C\frac{R}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}(1+|y|)^{2}}K,

where

K=\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}+\Bigl\|\frac{\omega}{fR}\Bigr\|_{\infty,T_{2}}.

Applying parabolic estimates to (
12.10
) and a scaling argument we find

(12.12)

\displaystyle|\nabla g_{0}(y,\tau)|\leq C\frac{RK}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}(1+|y|)^{3}}.

Using (
12.11
), (
12.12
) and
g_{0}=gU
we get that

\displaystyle|\nabla U\cdot\nabla g+g\Delta U|\leq C\frac{RK}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}(1+|y|)^{4}}.

We observe that for
i=1,2

(12.13)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla(U\nabla g)y_{i}\,dy=0.

Indeed,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla(U\nabla g)y_{i}\,dy

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U\nabla ge_{i}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g\nabla Ue_{i}.

But from
g=\frac{\phi}{U}-\psi
,
\psi=(-\Delta)^{-1}\phi
we have

\displaystyle-\Delta\psi-U\psi=Ug=g_{0}.

Multiplying this equation by
z_{i}=\nabla\Gamma_{0}e_{i}
defined in (
9.2
) and integrating we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}gU\nabla\Gamma_{0}e_{i}=0,

which is the desired claim (
12.13
).
We note that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} -\Delta v&=\nabla U\cdot\nabla g+g\Delta U=\nabla\cdot(g\nabla U)\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.\\
v(y)&\to 0\quad\text{as }|y|\to\infty.\end{aligned}\right.

Now we can apply Remark
9.1
and deduce that for any
\vartheta\in(0,1)
there is
C
such that

(12.14)

\displaystyle|v(y,\tau)|\leq C\frac{RK}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}(1+|y|)^{2-\vartheta}}.

We next estimate
\tilde{h}
. From Remark
9.1
and the assumptions on
h
, in particular (
12.4
), we have

(12.15)

\displaystyle|((-\Delta)^{-1}h)(y,\tau)|\leq C\frac{\|h\|}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}(1+|y|)^{2-\vartheta}},

for any
\vartheta\in(0,1)
.
Also from (
12.11
) we have

\displaystyle|Ug_{0}(y,\tau)|\leq C\frac{R}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}(1+|y|)^{6}}K.

Therefore, from (
12.15
), (
12.11
), (
12.14
) we find that for any
\vartheta>0

\displaystyle|\tilde{h}(y,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{RK}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\Bigl[\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{5+\sigma}}\min\Bigl(1,\frac{\tau^{\epsilon/2}}{\rho^{\epsilon}}\Bigr)+\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{6-\vartheta}}\Bigr].

We now use a barrier as in the proof of Lemma
10.8
, in a domain of the form
(\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B_{R_{0}})\times(\tau_{0},\infty)
where
R_{0}
is a large constant.
We let
\tilde{g}(y)
be the radial decaying solution to
-\Delta_{6}\tilde{g}=\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{5+\sigma}}
and

\displaystyle\bar{g}(y,\tau)=\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\tilde{g}(y)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{y}{\delta\sqrt{\tau}}\Bigr)+C_{1}\frac{1}{\tau^{\nu+\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\sigma}{2}}(\log\tau)^{m}}\Bigl[\frac{1}{(1+|y|/\sqrt{\tau})^{\mu}}+C_{2}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{4\tau}}\Bigr]

where

\displaystyle\mu=\min(5+\sigma+\epsilon,6-\vartheta).

We assume that
\nu<\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\sigma}{2}-\frac{\vartheta}{2}
,
\nu<1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}
,
\nu+\frac{\sigma}{2}<\frac{3}{2}
, and
\sigma+\vartheta<1
.
Since
\vartheta>0
is arbitrary we only need
\nu<\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\sigma}{2}
,
\nu<1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}
and
\sigma<1
.
Then, for an appropriate choice of
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
, the function
RK\bar{g}(y,\tau)
is a supersolution. By the maximum principle

\displaystyle|g_{0}(y,\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq CRK\bar{g}(y,\tau).

This proves the desired estimate (
12.7
).

∎

Next we consider equation (
8.3
), which we recall,

(12.16)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}\phi&=L[\phi]+B[\phi]+h\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\tau_{0},\infty)\\
\phi(\cdot,\tau_{0})&=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

For
\phi
with no radial mode we can write

B[\phi]=(\zeta_{1}(t)\phi+\zeta_{2}(t)y\cdot\nabla\phi)\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\lambda y}{5\sqrt{t}}\Bigr).

Corollary 12.1
.

Let
0<\sigma<1
,
0<\epsilon<2
,
1<\nu<\min(1+\frac{\epsilon}{2},\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\sigma}{2})
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then there is a
C>0
such that for any
\tau_{0}
sufficiently large the following holds.
Suppose that
h(y,\tau)
has no radial mode and satisfies
\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}<\infty
,

(12.17)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}h(y,\tau)y_{j}dy=0\quad\text{for all }\tau>\tau_{0},\quad j=1,2.

Then the solution
\phi(y,\tau)
of (
12.16
) satisfies

(12.18)

\displaystyle|\phi(y,\tau)|\leq C\frac{\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}}{\tau^{\nu}(\log\tau)^{m}}\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{3+\sigma}},&|y|\leq\sqrt{\tau}.\\
\frac{\tau^{1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}}{|y|^{5+\sigma+\epsilon}},&|y|\geq\sqrt{\tau}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Using Proposition
12.1
, there is a linear operator
T
so that given
h
with
\|h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}<\infty
, with no radial mode, and satisfying the condition (
12.17
) associates the solution
\phi
of (
12.3
). Then the solution
\phi
of (
12.16
) can be written as

\phi=T[B[\phi]+h].

The estimate (
12.5
) implies

\|\phi\|_{\nu,m,3+\sigma,2+\epsilon}\leq\|B[\phi]+h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}.

Using standard parabolic estimates we also get

\displaystyle\||y|\nabla\phi\|_{\nu,m,3+\sigma,2+\epsilon}\leq\|B[\phi]+h\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}.

Next we observe that

\displaystyle\|B[\phi]\|_{\nu,m,5+\sigma,\epsilon}\leq\frac{C}{\log\tau_{0}}\||\phi|+|y||\nabla\phi|\|_{\nu,m,3+\sigma,2+\epsilon}.

Then for
\tau_{0}
large we deduce the estimate (
12.18
).
∎

We are now in a position to prove Proposition
8.2
in the general case.

Proof of Proposition
8.2
.

We decompose
h=h_{0}+h_{1}
and
\phi=\phi_{0}+\phi_{1}
as in (
12.1
), (
12.2
).
We apply Proposition
11.1
to get

\displaystyle\|\phi_{0}\|_{\nu-\frac{1}{2},m+\frac{q}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}.

To estimate
\phi_{1}
we use Corollary
12.1
.
First we select
0<\vartheta<1
. Then note that

\displaystyle\|h_{1}\|_{\nu,m,6-\vartheta,\sigma+\epsilon+\vartheta}\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}.

Then by Corollary
12.1
we obtain a solution
\phi_{1}
of
(
12.16
) such that

\displaystyle\|\phi_{1}\|_{\nu,m,4-\vartheta,2+\sigma+\epsilon+\vartheta}\leq C\|h_{1}\|_{\nu,m,6-\vartheta,\sigma+\epsilon+\vartheta}.

This implies

\displaystyle\|\phi_{1}\|_{\nu-\frac{1}{2},m+\frac{q}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\|\phi_{1}\|_{\nu-\frac{\vartheta}{2},m+\frac{q}{2},4,2+\sigma+\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq C\|h\|_{\nu,m,6+\sigma,\epsilon}.

To apply Corollary
12.1
we need
1<\nu<1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}
and
\nu<1+\frac{\vartheta}{2}
. Given

1<\nu<\min(1+\frac{\epsilon}{2},3-\frac{\sigma}{2},\frac{5}{4})

we can select
\vartheta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
such that
\nu<1+\frac{\vartheta}{2}
and then proceed.
This concludes the proof.

∎

13.
The outer problem

We consider the linear outer problem:

(13.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\phi^{o}&=L^{o}[\phi^{o}]+g(x,t),\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(t_{0},\infty)\\
\phi^{o}(\cdot,t_{0})&=0,\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle L^{o}[\varphi]:=\Delta_{x}\varphi-\nabla_{x}\Big[\Gamma_{0}\Big(\frac{x-\xi(t)}{\lambda(t)}\Big)\Big]\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi=\Delta_{x}\varphi+4\frac{(x-\xi)}{|x-\xi|^{2}+\lambda^{2}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi.

For
g:\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(t_{0},\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
we consider the norm
\|g\|_{**,o}
defined as the least
K
such that for all

(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(t_{0},\infty)

\displaystyle|g(x,t)|\leq K\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{1+|\zeta|^{b}},\quad\zeta=\frac{x-\xi(t)}{\sqrt{t-t_{0}+A}},

where
A>0
is a constant.

We also define the norm
\|\phi\|_{*,o}
as the least
K
such that

\displaystyle|\phi^{o}(x,t)|+(\lambda+|x-\xi|)|{\nabla}_{x}\phi^{o}(x,t)|\leq K\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a-1}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{1+|\zeta|^{b}},\quad\zeta=\frac{x-\xi}{\sqrt{t-t_{0}+A}}

for all
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(t_{0},\infty)
.

We assume that the parameters
a,b
satisfy the constraints

(13.2)

\displaystyle 1<a<4,\quad 2<b<6,\quad a<1+\frac{b}{2}.

There is no restriction on
\beta
.

We recall from (
4.1
) that we are assuming that

(13.3)

\displaystyle|\dot{\lambda}(t)|\leq\frac{C}{t(\log t)^{3/2}},\quad t>t_{0},

and

(13.4)

\displaystyle|\dot{\xi}(t)|\leq\frac{C}{t^{\frac{3}{2}+\sigma}},\quad t>t_{0},

where
0<\sigma<\frac{1}{2}
.

Proposition 13.1
.

Assume that
a,b
satisfy (
13.2
),
\frac{A}{\lambda(t_{0})^{2}}
is sufficiently large, and
\lambda,\xi
satisfy (
13.3
), (
13.4
).
Then there is a constant
C
so that for
t_{0}
sufficiently large and for
\|g\|_{**,o}<\infty
there exists a solution
\phi^{o}=\mathcal{T}^{o}_{\textbf{p}}[g]
of (
13.1
), which defines a linear operator of
g
and satisfies

\displaystyle\|\phi^{o}\|_{*,o}\leq C\|g\|_{**,o}.

Proposition
6.3
in Section
6
follows from Proposition
13.1
with
A=t_{0}
.

Lemma 13.1
.

Let
2<\beta<6
and
h(r)
satisfy

(13.5)

\displaystyle|h(r)|\leq\frac{\lambda^{-2}}{(r/\lambda+1)^{\beta}}=\frac{\lambda^{\beta-2}}{(r+\lambda)^{\beta}},

where
\lambda>0
.
Then there is a unique bounded radial function
\varphi(r)
satisfying

\displaystyle L^{o}[\varphi]+h=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.

Moreover
\varphi
satisfies

(13.6)

\displaystyle|\varphi(r)|+(\lambda+r)|\partial_{r}\varphi(r)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+r/\lambda)^{\beta-2}}=C\frac{\lambda^{\beta-2}}{(r+\lambda)^{\beta-2}}

Proof.

The equation for
\varphi
is given by

\displaystyle\partial_{rr}\varphi(r)+\Bigl(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{4r}{\lambda^{2}+r^{2}}\Bigr)\partial_{r}\varphi(r)+h(r)=0,\quad r>0.

We change variables
\rho=\frac{r}{\lambda}
and let
\varphi(r)=\bar{\varphi}(\frac{r}{\lambda})
. Then we need to solve

\partial_{\rho\rho}\bar{\varphi}+\Bigl(\frac{1}{\rho}+\frac{4\rho}{1+\rho^{2}}\Bigr)\partial_{\rho}\bar{\varphi}+\bar{h}(\rho)=0,\quad\rho>0,

where

\bar{h}(\rho)=\lambda^{2}h(\lambda\rho).

By (
13.5
)

|\bar{h}(\rho)|\leq\frac{1}{(1+\rho)^{\beta}}.

The bounded solution is given by

\bar{\varphi}(\rho)=\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\frac{1}{v(1+v^{2})^{2}}\int_{0}^{v}\bar{h}(s)s(1+s^{2})^{2}\,ds\,dv.

By direct computation we get

|\bar{\varphi}(\rho)|+(1+\rho)|\partial_{\rho}\bar{\varphi}(\rho)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+\rho)^{\beta-2}},

and this implies (
13.6
).

∎

Proof of Proposition
13.1
.

To find a pointwise estimate for the solution
\phi^{o}
we construct a barrier.

Using polar coordinates
x-\xi(t)=re^{i\theta}
,

L^{o}
can be written as:

\displaystyle L^{o}[\varphi]=\partial_{rr}\varphi+\Bigl(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{4r}{\lambda^{2}+r^{2}}\Bigr)\partial_{r}\varphi+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta\theta}\varphi.

First we construct a function
\tilde{\psi}(r,t)
such that

\Bigl[\partial_{t}-\partial_{rr}-\Bigl(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{4r}{\lambda^{2}+r^{2}}\Bigr)\partial_{r}\bigr]\tilde{\psi}\geq\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{(1+r/\sqrt{t-t_{0}+A})^{b}}.

Let

\psi_{1}(r,t)=\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a-1}(\log t)^{\beta}}\Bigl[\frac{1}{(1+\frac{r^{2}}{t-t_{0}+A})^{b/2}}+C_{1}e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{4(t-t_{0}+A)}}\Bigr].

Choosing a large constant
C_{1}
,
\psi_{1}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\psi_{1}-\partial_{rr}\psi_{1}-\frac{5}{r}\partial_{r}\psi_{1}\geq c\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{(1+\frac{r}{\sqrt{t-t_{0}+A}})^{b}},\quad\text{for }r>0,\ t>t_{0},

where
c>0
.
Here we require
a<4
and
a<1+\frac{b}{2}
, which are part of the conditions (
13.2
).
Then

\displaystyle\Bigl[\partial_{t}-\partial_{rr}-\Bigl(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{4r}{\lambda^{2}+r^{2}}\Bigr)\partial_{r}\bigr]\psi_{1}

\displaystyle=\Bigl[\partial_{t}-\partial_{rr}-\frac{5}{r}\partial_{r}\Bigr]\psi_{1}+4\frac{\lambda^{2}}{r(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})}\partial_{r}\psi_{1}

(13.7)

\displaystyle\geq c\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{(1+\frac{r}{\sqrt{t-t_{0}+A}})^{b}}-4\frac{\lambda^{2}}{r(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})}|\partial_{r}\psi_{1}|.

But

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\psi_{1}=\frac{r}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a}(\log t)^{\beta}}\Bigl[-\frac{b}{(1+\frac{r^{2}}{t-t_{0}+A})^{b/2+1}}-\frac{C_{1}}{2}e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{4(t-t_{0}+A)}}\Bigr]

and so

(13.8)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}}{r(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})}|\partial_{r}\psi_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\lambda^{2}}{r^{2}+\lambda^{2}}\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{1}{(1+\frac{r^{2}}{t-t_{0}+A})^{b/2+1}}.

We note that for
r\leq\sqrt{t-t_{0}+A}
we have

(13.9)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}}{r(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})}|\partial_{r}\psi_{1}|\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}}{r^{2}+\lambda^{2}}\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a}(\log t)^{\beta}}\leq C\frac{\lambda^{2}}{(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})^{2}}\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a-1}(\log t)^{\beta}},

where we have used that
A\geq\lambda(t)^{2}
.

Let
\tilde{\psi}_{2}(r;\lambda)
be the bounded solution of

\displaystyle-\Bigl[\partial_{rr}+\Bigl(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{4r}{\lambda^{2}+r^{2}}\Bigr)\partial_{r}\Bigr]\tilde{\psi}_{2}=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})^{2}},\quad r>0,

given by Lemma
13.1
.
Then
\tilde{\psi}_{2}
can be written as

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{2}(r;\lambda)=\bar{\psi}_{2}\Bigl(\frac{r}{\lambda}\Bigr),

for a function
\bar{\psi}_{2}
satisfying

(13.10)

\displaystyle|\bar{\psi}_{2}(\rho)|+(1+\rho)|\bar{\psi}_{2}^{\prime}(\rho)|\leq\frac{C}{1+\rho^{2}}.

Let

\psi_{2}(r,t)=\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)t^{a-1}(\log t)^{\beta}}\tilde{\psi}_{2}(r;\lambda(t)).

Then, using (
13.10
) and (
13.3
), we get

\displaystyle\Bigl[\partial_{t}-\partial_{rr}-\Bigl(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{4r}{\lambda^{2}+r^{2}}\Bigr)\partial_{r}\bigr]\psi_{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a-1}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})^{2}}\Bigl[1-\Bigl(\frac{a-1}{t-t_{0}+A}+\frac{\beta}{t\log t}\Bigr)\tilde{\psi}_{2}(r)\frac{(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{\lambda}\bar{\psi}_{2}^{\prime}\Bigl(\frac{r}{\lambda}\Bigr)\frac{r}{\lambda}\frac{(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\geq\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a-1}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})^{2}}\Bigl[1-C\frac{r^{2}+\lambda^{2}}{t-t_{0}+A}\Bigr].

Therefore there is
\delta>0
(fixed independent of
t_{0}
) such that for all
t_{0}
large,

(13.11)

\displaystyle\Bigl[\partial_{t}-\partial_{rr}-\Bigl(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{4r}{\lambda^{2}+r^{2}}\Bigr)\partial_{r}\bigr]\psi_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a-1}(\log t)^{\beta}}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})^{2}},\quad\text{for }r\leq 2\delta\sqrt{t}.

Let
\chi_{0}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be such that
\chi_{0}(s)=1
if
s\leq 1
and
\chi_{0}(s)=0
if
s\geq 2
and define

\displaystyle\chi_{\delta}(r,t)=\chi_{0}\Bigl(\frac{r}{\delta\sqrt{t-t_{0}+A}}\Bigr).

We consider

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}=\psi_{1}+M\psi_{2}\chi_{\delta},

where
M>0
is a constant to be fixed later.
We compute, using (
13.7
)

\displaystyle\Bigl[\partial_{t}-\partial_{rr}-\Bigl(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{4r}{\lambda^{2}+r^{2}}\Bigr)\partial_{r}\bigr]\tilde{\psi}

\displaystyle\geq c\frac{1}{(t-t_{0}+A)^{a}(\log t)^{\beta}(1+\frac{r}{\sqrt{t-t_{0}+A}})^{b}}-4\frac{\lambda^{2}}{r(r^{2}+\lambda^{2})}|\partial_{r}\psi_{1}|

(13.12)
